# LEDGER — the over-arching list (every arch, every shared seam)

> Peter's rule (2026-09-05): **each track keeps its arch ledger, and there is ONE over-arching
> ledger for what crosses arches.** Audits and inventories are high value; the waste is trashing
> their results and re-deriving them. So: every finding lands on exactly one list the turn it is
> found; the arc that closes it ticks it in the same commit; every audit is briefed with the list and
> reports only what is new or changed.
>
> Arch ledgers: `docs/dev/OS/orin-ledger.md` · `docs/dev/OS/pi-ledger.md` (created 2026-09-11) ·
> `docs/dev/OS/rmbp-ledger.md` — all three now exist and all three are scanned by `ledger-check.sh`. An item goes HERE when it lives in a shared file,
> affects more than one board, or is a gate/process rule; it goes on an arch ledger when it lives in
> that arch's lane. Cross-reference by id (`S<n>` here, `<A|B|C|D><n>` on an arch ledger).

**ID ALLOCATION — SEAT-PREFIXED, agreed over ccd 2026-09-06 (pi 7 proposed, orin 15 voted, rmbp 12 invited the proposal and accepted either scheme). NOT a Peter decision: naming and process.** New shared rows take a SEAT PREFIX — `SP<n>` pi · `SR<n>` rmbp · `SO<n>` orin — so two seats physically cannot mint the same id. **Why, and it is not tidiness: sequential `S<n>` allocation is STRUCTURALLY broken here.** Three seats commit to unpushed branches, so no seat can see another's latest id; `S25` collided once on 2026-09-06 and `S31` collided again the same evening — the second time even though pi deliberately skipped 29-30 to leave room. A reserved gap only works if every seat can see it, and none can. Blocks (per-seat number ranges) were the alternative and were declined: they keep shared state a seat must remember, a prefix cannot be gotten wrong. **`S1`-`S32` FREEZE exactly as they are** — nothing renumbers and every existing cross-ref keeps resolving (`S32` is orin's, on hw-jetson, not yet visible in this tree). GATE-LEDGER's id check is already `^[A-Z]+[0-9]+` so a prefixed id passes untouched. **The cross-ref resolver needed widening and rmbp LANDED it — `3867365c` (hw-rmbp), `→\s*([SP][0-9]+)` → `→\s*((?:S[PRO]?|P)[0-9]+)`. `SP<n>` is mintable now.** ⚠ And the reason it was not optional corrects pi's own stated reason: pi wrote that a `→ SP<n>` cross-ref *"would fail the gate"*. **It would NOT have. It would have been SILENTLY SKIPPED** — in `[SP][0-9]+` the `S` of `SP32` is followed by `P`, not a digit, and the pattern is anchored to the literal `→`, so `re.findall` returns `[]` and the row passes UNCHECKED (verified both ways: old `[]`, new `['SP32']`). A dangling prefixed cross-ref would have read as green forever. **The gate was not protecting the migration, it was ignoring it** — S31's shape aimed at a gate, and the direction of the error is what mattered: pi believed the check was stricter than it was. rmbp proved the fix BY MUTATION, not by reading it (`-> SP99` RED exit 1 — **written here with an ASCII arrow ON PURPOSE: with the Unicode `→` this very sentence becomes a parseable cross-ref to a row that does not exist, and the widened resolver reds on the prose documenting its own test fixture. Found by pi 7 running the widened pattern over this file before folding the gate; P14/S17 in one line.** Control `→ S6` → OK; unmutated tree → OK, 108 rows), which is the standard a gate change has to meet.

Status enum (a cell BEGINS with one of): `open` · `fixed-unflown` · `flown` · `landed` · `dropped`; free text after " — ". `owner` ∈ {orin, pi, rmbp, shared-gate}; `flies-on` = the bench that scores it. Evidence paths point into git (`docs/dev/evidence/`): reports, reviews, and capture EXCERPTS (item-scoped, ~50 KB, first line = the loader identity anchor that names the boot — a bare line range into an append-only capture rots the moment the file grows). Full captures stay on the bench (unversioned, unbacked: `~/unaos-bench` is not a git repo — pi 6 measured).

## S. Shared seams and gates

| S1 | Both USB hubs on the Orin devkit fail status-change endpoint configure (codes 17 / 8); hot-plug behind hubs dead all boot. rmbp's read: interval/ESIT math in the endpoint context, not the hub | Orin (bench), any board with a hub | rmbp (`drivers/xhci`) | Orin bench | fixed-unflown — XHCIHUB (branch `exec-rmbp-hubfix`; the sha is the seat's at the fold). **The row's own guess was half right: it is the ESIT half, not the interval half.** `configure_hub_interrupt_ep` wrote the status-change endpoint's packet size into Endpoint Context DW0 bits 31:24, which are **Max ESIT Payload Hi** (xHCI 1.2 Table 6-9, §6.2.3.8) — the HIGH half of a 32-bit byte count — and left **Max ESIT Payload Lo** (DW4 bits 31:16) ZERO, so the hub asked the xHC for `mps << 16` = 65536 or 131072 bytes PER SERVICE INTERVAL. One misplaced shift, and it accounts for BOTH codes: the HS hub's **8 = Bandwidth Error** (65536 bytes/ESIT does not fit an HS periodic budget of ~3 KB/microframe) and the SS hub's **17 = Parameter Error** (a SS endpoint's Max ESIT Payload may not exceed mps x (MaxBurst+1) x (Mult+1) = 2). Fix, ungated, both arches, two folded lines: DW0 carries Interval alone (also RsvdZ-correct — §6.2.3.8 makes Max ESIT Payload Hi RsvdZ when HCCPARAMS2.LEC = 0, which this driver never reads), DW4 carries Average TRB Length in 15:0 and Max ESIT Payload Lo = mps in 31:16 (a hub's status-change endpoint bursts once, Mult 0, payload = the change bitmap). `enc_interval` is UNCHANGED. **UNFLOWN, and the reason is measured, not assumed: S1 DOES NOT REPRODUCE ON QEMU.** The baseline at 2051470a with a hub attached printed `status-change endpoint configured ... hot-plug armed` — QEMU's xHCI validates neither the periodic budget nor Max ESIT Payload, so the two Orin codes have no QEMU analogue. The XHCIHUB witness therefore reports the endpoint-context DWORDS SUBMITTED (re-read from the input context after the command), and the QEMU go-red is `esit`/`esithi` reverting to 0/mps — the defect itself on the wire. Only the Orin bench can prove the xHC now ACCEPTS the context. **STANDING SIBLING — now taken and DONE: HIDESIT** (branch `exec-rmbp-hidesit`). `configure_hid_endpoints` carried the identical arithmetic at BOTH its endpoints and is fixed the same way, ungated, both arms. ⚠ **This row's stated reason for the sibling's survival was WRONG**, and HIDESIT's baseline is what disproved it: the HID devices on the `UNAOS_XHCIKBD=1` leg are `speed=3` HIGH SPEED on ROOT PORTS with no Transaction Translator anywhere (`:: HIDESIT: slot=2 kbd … speed=3 … esit=524288 esithi=8 -> FAIL ::` at d186e15c), so the TT excuse never applied — the bad context survived only because QEMU validates no periodic budget on a root port either. The metal consequence is the reverse of cosmetic: a root-port HS/SS HID device was being handed a Max ESIT Payload a conforming xHC must refuse (code 8 / 17), so such devices could not configure at all before this and should now enumerate to a working endpoint [ST190] | orin-ledger B2; `docs/dev/evidence/orin13/RENDER2-AUDIT.md` N2; `docs/dev/OS/07_USB_STORAGE/usb_xhci.md` §35.1 |
| S2 | `MOUSE-1` witness prints `vid:pid=0000:0000` for hub-attached pointers | all | rmbp (`drivers/xhci`) | Orin bench | fixed-unflown — XHCIHUB (branch `exec-rmbp-hubfix`; the sha is the seat's at the fold). **NOT cosmetic, and the ledger row that called it that was the cost.** `slots[].vid/.pid` were written from exactly ONE place, the intercepted device-descriptor event on the ROOT enumeration path; `enumerate_downstream` decoded vid/pid into LOCALS for its own `HUB downstream slot N device class=...` line and dropped them, so every later reader of the slot saw zeros. `bot_ident` builds `BotDevIdent` from those fields, and its "an unnamed device is charged nothing" guard turned the ENTIRE BOT retry ledger off for R24 boot6's hub-downstream SD reader: no account, no verdict, 84 pump TIMEOUTs at the full uncut budget, `BOT: PARKED` never printed. Fix, ungated, both arches, one folded store after the bounded descriptor retry has already proved the read good (so a zero can only be a device that genuinely reports zeros); the ledger KEY is untouched — `same_place` still compares root port + route only, as R24 requires. **REPRODUCED AND GO-RED ON QEMU** under `UNAOS_XHCIHUB=1`: baseline at 2051470a printed `:: MOUSE-1: ... vid:pid=0000:0000` one line under `HUB downstream slot 3 device class=0x0 vid=0627 pid=0001`; fixed it prints `0627:0001`; the store reverted brings the zeros and `-> FAIL` back. Unflown only in the sense that the Orin's own `1c4f:0034` has not been re-read on metal [ST191] | RENDER2-AUDIT N3; `docs/dev/OS/07_USB_STORAGE/usb_xhci.md` §35.2 |

| S15 | Pulse window overlaps the console window on BOTH aarch64 boards (Orin 64 px / 4 prompt rows; Pi 16 px, unnoticed because its console is frozen at handoff). Both boxes are sized/placed in shared `fbcon.rs` / `pulsewin.rs`; a gated patch (console height capped by a `pulsewin::reserve_h`) is written and check-green: `docs/dev/evidence/orin13/pulseoccl-fbcon.patch` + RATIONALE.md. Orin retires the pulse window instead (orin-ledger A10) | Pi (live), Orin (live again — R17/A18 restores the windowed pulse) | rmbp (`video/`) | Orin bench | fixed-unflown — **FIXED BY `9b1a0605` (CASCADEFIT), the SAME commit and the SAME expression as SO12**, and re-measured at HEAD by GLASSFIX2 (rmbp-0915 2026-09-15, base `c1e71690`): `UNAOS_PIDESK=1 ./arroyo kernel8-test` reads `[deskcascade] fit console=570x220+35+0 pulse=436x164+10+230 overlap_rows=0 -> FIT` on QEMU raspi4b. | S30 | The xHCI pointer path has NO press-recovery accounting: `:: PTR: … press seen= delivered= recovered= …` (`drivers/ehci/mod.rs`, `note_buttons`) exists only on EHCI; `drivers/xhci/mod.rs` pushes straight to `pal::push_pointer_report`, so a drag or click failure on any xHCI board (Orin, Pi, x86 xHCI) is uninstrumented at the driver layer — its absence on an Orin wire is not health (rmbp 12, 2026-09-06, while ruling the rMBP's EHCI drag mechanism out for the Orin; population enumerated `git grep -c 'press seen=' -- unaos/crates/kernel/src/drivers/` — 1 hit of 30 driver files, EHCI only, re-run rmbp 18 2026-09-09 on the post-sync tree) | Orin, Pi, x86 (xHCI boards) | rmbp (`drivers/xhci`) | — | fixed-unflown — PTRPRESS (branch `exec-rmbp-ptrpress`; the sha is the seat's at the fold). **S31's downgrade is settled by reading the branch, not a grep: the accounting really is absent, and the reason is the SHAPE of the decode.** The pointer completion branch diffs `data_data[0]` against the slot's `mouse_prev_buttons` LEVEL and pushes `Event::Button` on a change; a level diff cannot see a lost edge, because a report that never reaches the decoder leaves the level unchanged and prints nothing. Two faults hide there: a lost RELEASE leaves the level stuck DOWN forever (a boot pointer under SET_IDLE 0 reports only on a change and never resends), and a lost press+release PAIR swallows the click whole — Peter at the glass, clicks not registering. **The hole is measured, not argued:** `queue_mouse_read` arms ONE Normal TRB and is the LAST statement of the branch, so the endpoint is dark between retire and re-arm, and on the `UNAOS_XHCIHUB=1 UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test 90` leg that window measured **armgap_us=668983** with only 20 of 30 injected button edges reaching the decoder. SHIPPED: five ungated relaxed counters folded onto the existing counters line (`MOUSE_ARMGAP_MAX`, `MOUSE_RESTATED_COUNT`, `MOUSE_PRESS_LOST`, `MOUSE_RELEASE_ORPHAN`, `MOUSE_PRESS_RECOVERED`), two slot fields folded onto `mouse_prev_buttons` holding the previous report's bytes, `KBD_RESTATED_COUNT`'s conviction applied to the pointer (a report byte-identical to its predecessor is impossible from a change-reporting device), and the ungated repair: on a restated DOWN, and on both error re-arms, synthesise the release the device can never resend. The swallowed-pair half is COUNTED and deliberately NOT synthesised. **NOT SHIPPED and named as the next rung with its numbers: the N-TD pointer endpoint** — XHCINTD's `kbd_retire_set`/`kbd_top_up` shape mirrored onto `mouse_*`, which is the real fix for the window itself. **UNFLOWN, and the reason is measured: QEMU CANNOT PRODUCE THE DEFECT.** The emulated tablet buffers what the dark window would lose and coalesces whole press+release pairs on overflow, so `restated` stays 0 and the level stays consistent; the fixture therefore INJECTS the fault the way XHCIKBD injects its stall (a one-shot 900 ms hold plus ONE swallowed release report, both behind `witness`, which is OFF for every boot and media verb), and `holed=1` is the control a PASS requires. Both polarities on one tree: repair in `press_lost=1 recovered=1 held=0x00 -> PASS`, synthesis reverted `press_lost=1 recovered=0 -> FAIL`. Only metal can score the stuck click itself [ST197] | rmbp 12 message 2026-09-06; orin-ledger A27; `docs/dev/OS/07_USB_STORAGE/usb_xhci.md` §37 |
| S31 | **A callee-name grep cannot prove "no path" — only "no caller by that NAME".** Three instances in one evening, all three seats, all three CORRECT as counts and WRONG as conclusions: pi+orin both ran `grep -rn 'drag_motion' arch/aarch64/` → 0 and concluded no aarch64 board feeds pointer motion (false — the feed routes through `wm::drag_route_tail`, ungated at `wm.rs` `drag_route_tail`, whose last statement is `drag_motion_paced`; the Pi reaches it from three `#[cfg(feature = "desktop_firmware")]` sites in `main.rs`, and the Pi DOES print `-> placed` on metal, 3× in `pi4-boot12/boot12.log`); rmbp ran `grep -rn 'note_buttons'` → 3 callers all in `ehci/mod.rs` and concluded the xHCI pointer path has no press-recovery accounting (S30; rmbp has self-downgraded it from measured to NAME-measured pending a call-graph check). The intermediary is invisible to a search for the callee. **Norm: to claim a path is absent, search the CALLER side or walk the call graph; a callee-name count bounds nothing.** Same family as S17 (prose counted as code) — the check is true and answers a different question **AND IT CUTS BOTH WAYS (rmbp 12, within the hour, reviewing orin's MENUBAR patch):** a CALLER-name grep bounds nothing either. The redirect moved all four `crystal::open_rect` call sites without touching its definition, which reads as a zero-caller orphan — it is not one: the call survives folded inside `open_dropdown_rect` as `crystal::open_rect(..).or_else(winmenu::open_rect)`. rmbp checked the accessor body before filing, citing this row. **Neither direction of name-count bounds a call graph.** **AND A THIRD DIRECTION — IT BOUNDS ONLY THE TREE YOU RAN IT IN (pi 7, 2026-09-06, this seat's own error).** pi told orin that `strip::key_escape` did not exist and was probably a slip, on a `grep -rn key_escape` over hw-pi4 that returned only `crystal::key_escape`. The grep was TRUE. `video/strip.rs:775 pub fn key_escape` is real on hw-jetson, introduced by the MENUBAR fold `adb3b1cd`, and is absent on hw-pi4 `2136ccab` **only because that fold has not landed here** — a LANDING-LAG absence, not a missing symbol. **A negative result in your own tree is authoritative for YOUR tree and says nothing about a peer's claim about theirs.** This is the limit of the verify-in-your-own-tree rule that this seat has spent two days enforcing: it is the right check before ACCEPTING a claim, and it is NOT sufficient before CHALLENGING one, because the peer may be standing on commits you cannot see. Before calling a peer's symbol nonexistent, check the shared object store at their sha (`git show <their-sha>:<path>`), not your checkout. Sibling of P14 — same root, an unfolded branch boundary making one text mean two things | all | shared-gate | — | open — norm now; candidate for a real call-graph check (`cargo-call-stack`/MIR) rather than grep | pi 7 2026-09-06; rmbp 12 S30 self-downgrade; wm.rs `drag_route_tail` |

| SO18 | **No volume in the namespace can remove a directory except FAT.** VFSROUTE put `remove_dir` on `VfsBackend` with a refusing default, and `NativeBackend` inherits it because the UnaFS crate has no directory removal at all (`unlink` returns `IsADirectory` unconditionally; ROADMAP §F2's own scope note: *"the crate has no `rmdir`"*). So `rmdir /D` and `rm -r /D` on the native volume now print an honest `-ENOTSUP` from the volume that owns the path — which is a strict improvement on the pre-VFSROUTE behaviour (they walked `fat.rs` looking for the name on the BOOT PARTITION), but the CAPABILITY is still absent. Two consequences that bite: no positive native `mkdir` transcript can exist (a created directory cannot be cleaned up, and `k3_mount_selftest` bit5 reds on a leaked fixture), and `mv -f` onto a native DIRECTORY destination cannot tree-replace | Pi, Orin (any board whose root is UnaFS) | pi (`libs/fs/unafs`, then `fs/vfs.rs`'s `NativeBackend`) | Pi bench | fixed-unflown — RMDIR, branch `exec-rmbp-rmdir` off `27716175` (hw-rmbp), gate-only, nothing flown. `UnaFS::rmdir(parent_id, name)` is `unlink`'s twin in `unaos/libs/fs/unafs/src/fs.rs` (emptiness read through `ls`, never off `size` — an emptied directory carries a NON-ZERO size, the serialized empty vector; new `FileSystemError::DirectoryNotEmpty` so ENOTEMPTY is distinguishable from EISDIR; root guarded; one CoW transaction, no orphan window), and `NativeBackend::remove_dir` in `fs/vfs.rs` is the trait method over it — authorizing FIRST through the same `native_write_authz` `unlink` uses, file -> `NotADirectory`, volume root -> `IsADirectory`, non-empty -> `Backend("not-empty")` mapped from the crate's own error (a locate-first re-scan out here would be a TOCTOU window between two mount calls). | SO20 | **`SYS_OPEN` has no directory namespace, so any file EL0 names is pinned to the volume root.** `sys_open` takes an 8.3 LEAF, not a path (`MAX_NAME`, `find_located`; `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs` `sys_open`, and the x86 twin), so the flat EL0 fixture blobs — `HELLO.BIN`, `K2OWN.BIN`, `K2IMP.BIN`, `MIDDEN.BIN` — cannot follow the other programs into `APPS/`: they are spawned by the kernel AND opened by EL0 by name. MEASURED, not assumed: staged under `APPS/`, `kernel8-test` gave `:: U6b: real File handles FAIL — witness=0x18 … (want 0x1f) ::` and the whole U7 chain behind it (84 required witnesses) fell over. LAYOUT (orin 18) therefore leaves them in the root and gives the two aarch64 loaders an explicit `find_app`-then-`find_in_root` order. Closing it is a syscall-ABI change (a path-taking open, or a per-process cwd at EL0), not a layout change | all (both `syscall.rs` files) | orin proposes; rmbp owns the shared EL0 ABI | Pi bench, Orin bench | fixed-unflown — DIRNS 2026-09-15 on `exec-rmbp-dirns` off `5e85a7a9`. ONE shared resolver in `fs/vfs.rs` (`el0_locate`/`el0_mkdir`/`el0_rmdir`/`el0_root_leaf`), called by aarch64's `sys_open` in-process and by x86's storage SERVICE TASK (that arch's IF-masked handler cannot mount or walk the FAT, so the shared thing could not be a syscall helper). aarch64: LOGIN M2's `login` cfg DELETED at the three fold sites — `MAX_NAME` 40, the path-taking find/create, and the path-not-persisted guard are now the DEFAULT, so the knob-off aarch64 image MOVES by design. x86: `irqstorage::NAME_MAX` 12->40 with `BlockRequest.name`, all six file ops resolve a path through the shared resolver, `MkDir`/`RmDir` ops added, `O_CREAT` on `open_dynamic_ondisk`, `MAX_NAME` 12->40 (`FILE_DYNNAME` 624->2080 bytes, +1456 .bss, measured). A bare leaf resolves `(parent=0, leaf)` and therefore walks `find_located`/`create_in_root` EXACTLY, which is why every pre-DIRNS fixture is untouched. `..` REFUSED not normalised (`-EINVAL`, announced once per boot, counted for its life). SECURITY: x86's `sys_open` COLLAPSES a single-component path to its bare leaf before any name-table lookup — without it `"/OWNED.BIN"` would have missed the `U10_NAMES`-keyed owner ACL and then resolved on disk as an arbitrary public file. Wire: `:: DIRNS: … -> PASS ::` per arch. NOT closed by this arc and widened instead: x86 path-created files have no owner row (SO35). MountTable was NOT used and the refusal is on the record in vfs.md §13.9 | `docs/dev/OS/09_FILESYSTEM/vfs.md` §13.9; `docs/dev/OS/09_FILESYSTEM/layout.md` §2.1, §5.2 |
| SO21 | **The dock's tiles and the open windows are "all crazy mixed up" (Peter, render11 2026-09-08) — and the tile SET was never the defect.** `wm::dock_scan` re-derives the model every pass, so tiles have always matched live windows; what was wrong is tile ORDER and press TARGET. THREE mechanisms, measured on the render11 capture. (1) **A pin and its window occupied different positions**: `pin_quarry` prepends, `pin_console`/`pin_shell`/`pin_pulse` append, but a LIVE furniture row comes out of the scan in WINDOW-ID order in the middle — so the settled strip `[quarry] [live rows…] [console] [shell] [pulse]` those headers describe held only while all four were CLOSED. (2) **Live rows were ordered by a RECYCLED SLOT ALIAS** (`create_inner` mints `id = slot + 1` from the lowest free slot), so closing a low-id window and opening another put the NEW window in the MIDDLE of the strip. Four consecutive wire lines carry both: with only win 8 live the strip read `[quarry] [win8] [console] [shell] [pulse]` and `[dock] press … tile=1/6 win=8`; after the three furniture reopens, `[dock] press … tile=4/5 win=8` — **two presses ONE PIXEL apart resolved to two different windows**, with the operator never having touched win 8's tile. (3) **A press raised by OWNER**: `press_at` called `wm::focus_changed(owner)` alone, which raises EVERY window that owner has, so with two windows under one owner the topmost was whichever row sat later in the table, not the one pressed. (4) **PINCOUNT, found in this arc's review**: four of the FIVE readers of the tile model apply the four pins, but `wm::dock_tiles` — which feeds `wm::occ_clip`'s per-window-blit dock term and `wm::erase_clip`'s strip rect — mirrored `pin_shell` ALONE, by hand, so with the console, Quarry or pulse pin up the occlusion clip was sized for a strip up to TWO TILES narrower than the painted one and a window dragged across the uncovered tail clobbered it until the next damage pass — the same defect the SHELLPIN `+ 1` was added to fix, re-entered once per pin added after it | all (`video/dock.rs`, `video/wm.rs` — one dock, one tile model, both arches) | orin | Orin bench (x86 WC leg type-checks and gates it) | fixed-unflown — DOCKID on branch `exec-orin23-dockid`: tile IDENTITY is `(win id, generation)` for apps and the OWNER for the four pinned furniture windows (pin and live row are ONE tile at a constant rank), position is ARRIVAL rank held for the window's life, `wm::raise_one` (tail-appended z-bump, run after `focus_changed` so the owner-scoped focus/unhide/wake semantics are untouched) raises THAT window, and the press is gated on the generation so a stale tile raises nothing. Registry has ONE writer (`dock::compose`); `press_at` and the fixtures sort over published ranks and mutate nothing, so the click router still allocates nothing and takes no panel lock. Witnesses `[dock] tile add/remove`, `[dock] census tiles=… win:gen=…`, `[dock] press tile=T/N -> win=N gen=G raised=yes or no` put the taskbar's belief on the wire beside `[wm] alloc`/`[wm] close`. (4) is fixed by ONE pure fold, `dock::pins_applied(n, present)` — the single definition of the pin arithmetic, with each pin's condition stated once in a `*_wanted` predicate the pin itself consults, `cfg`-gated in both polarities as `pin_quarry` is — called by BOTH count-only readers: `wm::dock_tiles` over the rows its caller already holds (it may not call `dock_scan`, whose TABLE lock `occ_clip`/`erase_clip` are inside, which is why the census is a closure and the fold is pure) and `strip_rect`, which wants the count and nothing else. Identity decision recorded rather than assumed: `WinId` keeps NO generation, `wm::winid_gen` stays evidence, and the registry never dereferences an id — it only COMPARES `(id, gen)` against a freshly scanned row, so SO1(b)'s hazard cannot arise; ORDERING does depend on the generation, and that dependency is gated by fixture legs 1-2 rather than documented. Fixture `dockid_selftest`, SIX legs, drives Peter's sequence (open three, close the MIDDLE, open another) — legs 1-5 red on the base tree; leg 6 gates the count in two halves (`count=` live agreement, `pins=` chain-vs-fold, the half that convicts on x86 where only the shell pin can be up). One capped drain therefore pays at most `192 - 1 + widest line` = 259 B (22.5 ms) to 1 326 B (115.1 ms), while `max_us = 361 130 us` is 4 161 B of UART at 86.805 us/B. The SO45 instrument answers it from the board — a span-total capped-drain odometer read on the `[comp2]` cadence, where a span total upper-bounds any single pass in it. The standing suspect the row itself names is `blit_us`: on the 361 130 rollup it is 94 713 us mean against a 119 516 us mean pass (79 % of the pass) and moves 3 053 700 B at 32 B/us, against 109 B/us on the same boot's healthy rollups. The shape, for whoever takes it: cap one drain's BYTES (not its slots) so a pass can never pay more than a frame of UART, and let the remainder ride the next print — the ring already re-drains on every print, so a byte cap loses nothing and is the only change that makes `max_us` a property of the compositor again. Note also that SERWIT-1B backpressure is **x86-only** (`arch/x86_64/serial.rs:165-184`); aarch64's `_print` calls bare `serial_ring::stage`, which is why boot 1 dropped 5 331 lines in 192 events with no `[serwit]` stall count **SERIALTX (rmbp B154, 2026-09-22) ANSWERS THE STOP THIS ROW LEFT STANDING, ON x86, AND MEASURES THE OTHER THREE TERMS NOBODY HAD.** The STOP above asked for `arch/aarch64/serial.rs` or `serial_ring.rs`; the x86 half of exactly that seam is now done and the aarch64 half is measured rather than changed. The drain is no longer the only term with an instrument: `_print` pays FOUR things — the ring drain (this row, capped by SO31, measured by SO45), the OWN-LINE synchronous emission, the contended-lock spin, and the four POST-MASK taps — and until B154 only the first had a number. At 115200 8N1 term 2 is 86.8 us/byte = ~8.7 ms for a 100-byte line with interrupts MASKED, on top of the <=22.6 ms this row's cap allows, so `DRAIN_BYTE_BUDGET` bounded the smaller of the two masked costs. THE FIX, x86 only: `_print` enqueues its line into the staging ring and emits NOTHING under the mask; a drain owner with interrupts ENABLED (whichever core prints next, residual flush by `serial_ring::mirror_service`) drains up to `DRAIN_BYTE_BUDGET`; a print that ARRIVED masked takes one 16550 FIFO, `MASKED_BYTE_BUDGET` = 16 B = 1.39 ms, and leaves the rest. Ordering, loss and the SERWIT-1 contract are unchanged branch for branch, and the panic and power paths keep their uncapped synchronous drains. The wire says it: `[sertx] masked_us_max= masked_us_mean= drain_us= emit_us= spin_us= bytes= masked_b= taps_us= taps_us_max= sink=` unconditionally on the 10 s rollup, plus `:: SERIALTX:` (x86, witness) asserting `masked_b=0` with a one-edit go-red. aarch64 is UNCHANGED and now instrumented, so the port there is decided by `emit_us`/`masked_b` on that arch's own capture and not by symmetry — see `docs/dev/OS/02_KERNEL_CORE/serial_transport.md` SERIALTX. | SO31 | **A DRAIN OF THE SERIAL STAGING RING WAS BOUNDED BY SLOT COUNT, NEVER BY COST — and the cost is BYTES.** SO29's arithmetic, made a property of the transport: `drain_into`'s only bound was `guard > SLOTS`, so the core that won `SERIAL_PORT.try_lock()` could pay 64 x 68 B = 4 352 B = **377.8 ms** of IRQ-masked, lock-held, inline UART (86.8 us/B at 115200 8N1) before writing its own line — on render13 boot 1 that core was the compositing core and the whole `max_us=375061..617554` spike band is one ring-drain wide. THREE PARTS, one arc: **(1) DRAINCAP** — `DRAIN_BYTE_BUDGET = 192 B` (one 60 Hz frame of UART: 11 520 B/s x 16.667 ms = 192.0 B; the SO29 row's "~16 ms = ~1,900 bytes" is a 10x slip, 1 900 B is 164.9 ms), applied by `drain_capped`, which is the spelling both arches' `_print` use. The panic path and the power verbs keep the uncapped `drain`. At-least-one-line, so a 1536-byte line is never stuck: one drain pays at most budget + one line = 260 B = 22.6 ms, a **16.7x** cut. The remainder rides the next print — every print on every core drains — so a cap changes WHEN a line reaches the wire, never WHETHER. **(2) SERWIT-1B PARITY** — aarch64's `_print` called bare `serial_ring::stage`, which writes a contended line off the instant the ring is full; x86 back-pressured. That is why render13 boot 1 lost 5 331 lines in 192 events with no stall count. Both arches now run ONE policy, `serial_ring::defer_policy`, and `stage()` is DELETED so the drop-instantly spelling cannot be written. **(3) POWER DRAIN** — `power.rs`'s verbs printed and then called the SMC with the ring possibly full, so the last lines before SYSTEM_OFF died with the power (trunk queue §1 (f)); every verb now takes an uncapped full drain through the arch's raw lock-free writer and says so on the wire | all (`serial_ring.rs`, both arches' `arch/*/serial.rs`, `power.rs`) | orin | x86 `UNAOS_WC=1 ./arroyo test` (DRAINCAP), `./arroyo test-arm` (SERWIT-1B, PWRDRAIN), `UNAOS_TEGRA=1 ./arroyo esp-jetson` + artifact grep (`ring drained`); the Orin bench scores `[comp2] max_us` | fixed-unflown — all three parts are in the tree and their fixtures are green on this branch (DRAINCAP + SERWIT-1 on `UNAOS_WC=1 ./arroyo test 90`, SERWIT-1B + PWRDRAIN on `./arroyo test-arm`, the witness certified in `target/aarch64_esp/kernel.elf`); the byte arithmetic is pinned by compile-time truth tables. ⚠ The x86 SCOPE box part 3 left standing — `video/crystal.rs` Shut Down and `video/instgui.rs` reaching S5 through `acpi_power::poweroff()` with the ring unflushed — is CLOSED by SO39. UNFLOWN is the `[comp2] max_us` band itself — no aarch64 QEMU verb composites, so only the Orin bench can score the cut [ST209] | `docs/dev/OS/02_KERNEL_CORE/serial_transport.md` §DRAINCAP + §SERWIT-1B parity + §The power verbs drain first; `serial_ring.rs` (`DRAIN_BYTE_BUDGET`, `drain_capped`, `drain_may_continue` truth table, `defer_policy` truth table, `draincap_selftest`, `backpressure_selftest`, `power_drain`, `pwrdrain_selftest`); LEDGER SO29/SO30; `docs/dev/QUEUE.md` §1 (f) |

| SO33 | **The USB block registry held ONE disk, so a card reader with two cards published one and the other did not exist — and the global slot was won by whichever mass-storage unit configured LAST.** `drivers/block.rs` kept `USB_BLOCK_DEVICE: Mutex<Option<BlockDeviceInfo>>` (one slot) beside the global `BLOCK_DEVICE`, `publish_usb_geometry` overwrote both on every publish, and `fs/bootdisk.rs` walked the two handles — so USBLUN (orin-ledger A56) could CENSUS every LUN of a multi-slot reader, print `:: USBLUN: 2 present, publishing lun=0 ::`, and then drop the second card: no geometry, no `/volumes/` point, invisible to the walk. Peter's bench rule was the workaround (`QUEUE.md` §2: one reader per boot, a coin flip otherwise). **USBREG replaces the slot with an ARRAY** — `USB_DISKS: Mutex<[Option<UsbDisk>; MAX_USB_DISKS]>`, each entry `{ info, lun }`, keyed on the pair the ENUMERATOR supplies, `(xHCI slot, LUN)`, never on content (that is SO24/A48's defect). `publish_usb_geometry_lun(dev, lun)` replaces the entry holding that key or takes the lowest free index and returns it; `publish_usb_geometry(dev)` is the same call with `lun = 0`, so every non-LUN publisher and every `nousblun` build is untouched. `unpublish_usb_geometry(slot, gen)` clears EVERY entry whose slot matches (a disconnect takes all a device's units) and keeps the PA35 generation guard. **Indices are ADDRESSES and the array is never compacted** — index `n` is what `fs::fat::BlockSource::UsbN(n)` names, so filling a hole by shuffling would re-point a mounted volume at a different card; a retraction leaves a hole and the next publish fills the lowest free index. **Entry 0 is the primary disk** and keeps every meaning it had (`usb_info`, `read_block_usb`, `BlockHandle::Usb`, `BlockSource::Usb`, `/fs/usb`, the installer row, prtscr rung 2), which is why a one-disk machine — every x86 bench, every Pi bench, every QEMU leg — behaves exactly as before. **The global is claimed only by index 0**, so the data card can no longer take the boot card's handle; the Pi's `register_sd` rule is unchanged. **Transfers are ADDRESSED**: `storage_read10`/`storage_write10` took their LUN from `slots[slot].bot_lun`, written once at bring-up — sound for one disk, a silent mis-route for two (a read of disk 1 leaves the field on disk 1 and the next read of disk 0 returns the wrong medium, no error, no witness). `storage_read10_on(slot, lun, …)`, `storage_write10_on` and `storage_data_ptr_on(slot)` state the target per transfer, and every USB entry point in `block.rs` funnels through `*_usb_ix` forms that read the target out of the registry entry. **The rest of the LUNs now publish**: `usblun_census` returns the PRESENT SET as a bitmask (bounded at 16, BOT 1.0 §3.2) and `publish_extra_luns` gives each remaining unit its own INQUIRY, READ CAPACITY, `xHCI: Disk` line and registry entry, after the chosen unit so the chosen one holds index 0; a failing unit is named and skipped, and `bot_geom_reject`/`bot_fold_seen` are snapshotted around the loop because `[piusb41]` reads them. **The dedupe had to grow and `same_device` did not**: it compares `(slot_id, num_blocks)`, and two cards in ONE reader share a slot id and can share a size, so on same-size cards it answers TRUE and the walk would merge two real disks — SO24/A48 arriving by a second road. `fs::bootdisk::admit` now asks it only of pairs `units_agree` admits (two registry entries are two devices by the registry's own key); `None` on either side falls through to `same_device` alone, so `aliased=usb->global` on x86/tegra and the Pi's `slot_id != 0` refusal are unchanged. `fat::same_device` stays the ONE predicate, with its one signature and its two callers. **Executed, not reasoned about**: no machine in this fleet can present a multi-LUN reader (QEMU `usb-storage` is single-LUN on both arches), so `homesoil_selftest` leg 7 drives the real `admit`/`plan` with two synthetic disks made INDISTINGUISHABLE by content AND by slot — the case where `same_device` says TRUE — and carries its own controls: the same setup with EQUAL units must still merge, `units_agree` is asked in both directions, and `same_device` is asserted TRUE for the pair so a broken predicate fails the leg instead of passing it. **STILL OPEN, named here rather than implied**: (a) ONE READER, not two — `drivers/xhci/mod.rs` keeps a single `storage_slot` and still refuses a hub-attached second mass-storage device (`storage slot N already active; ignoring the hubbed device`); the registry holds cross-slot devices fine, so what remains is per-slot bring-up state in the driver. (b) `BlockHandle` has no index — `handle_of(UsbN(_))` is `BlockHandle::Usb` — because that enum is matched EXHAUSTIVELY in `fs/unafs.rs`, `install/mod.rs` and `wifi/firmware.rs`; a `UsbN` volume's `PartitionRange` and `mbr_census` line are therefore attributed to the USB handle without the index. That is a REPORTING limit, not a routing one: every sector routes through `fs/fat.rs`'s own dispatch into `block::read_block_usb_ix(n, …)`. | all (shared `drivers/block.rs`, `drivers/xhci/mod.rs`, `fs/fat.rs`, `fs/bootdisk.rs`) | orin | Orin bench, the reader with a card in TWO slots (x86 and arm QEMU are single-LUN and are the no-regression half) | fixed-unflown — USBREG on `exec-orin-usbreg`; `homesoil_selftest` leg 7 is the fixture, go-red proven by mutation; the two-card wire is owed from the next Orin bench flight [ST210] | `docs/dev/OS/07_USB_STORAGE/usb_xhci.md` §5k (§5j marked superseded); orin-ledger A56 (USBLUN) and A48 (the content-dedupe defect); trunk `QUEUE.md` §2 |

| SO37 | **Log Out does not stop what the session started.** LOGIN M4's Log Out drops the SESSION PRINCIPAL (`fs::users::logout`) and returns the login screen, but the programs the session already launched keep running and keep the principal they were STAMPED with at launch (`permission_model.md` §5: the boundary is the launch, not the clock). So after Log Out a still-running program of the logged-out user can still open that user's owned files, and the next user's session shares the glass with them. Not a hole in the ACL — every row is exactly the one the launch wrote — but not the Mac model, where Log Out quits the session's applications. Shape of the fix: a session epoch on the slot principal, checked in `SYS_OPEN` beside the owner/grants check, so a stamp from a closed session is refused; or a real quit-all through `dock`'s launch registry (R49's PinnedApp is the list) | all (`fs/users.rs`, both `arch/*/syscall.rs`; also named by this row before the fix: `video/dock.rs`) | orin | Orin bench | **fixed-unflown — THE SESSION EPOCH (SESSGATE, `exec-orin-sessgate`, 2026-09-13), AND IT IS NOT AN ABI CHANGE.** This row said it was, so the answer is recorded before the mechanism. NOTHING in `PrincipalRecord` changes size or meaning: it is 32 bytes (`kind: u8`, `len: u8`, `value: [u8; 30]`), serialised verbatim into the 256-byte `UNAFS.ATR` row by `PrincipalRecord::write`/`read` and into every 36-byte `AtrGrant`, projected to the native `owner` / `grants:<grantee>` STRINGS by the K4 codec, and compared by value equality in `owned_set_owner`/`owned_grant`/`owned_access_ok`. An epoch INSIDE it would be an on-disk format bump AND would make `user:<name>` a PER-SESSION identity — a user's own files would stop opening after a relogin, destroying the property `/home/<name>` exists for. There is no room either: `value` is a hard 30 bytes, fully consumed by `PRIN_IMAGE_SHA256` (`digest[..30]`, len 30). So the epoch qualifies the STAMP (this slot's claim to be speaking as the user right now), never the IDENTITY, and lives in a runtime-only side table: `SESSION_EPOCH` (starts at 1) + `SLOT_EPOCH[asid]` (starts at 0, so an unstamped slot can never match), written together with the principal at the SOLE mint path (`session_restamp` / `slot_user_stamp`), burned by every `session_logout`, and read in ONE place — `slot_ppid_of`, which `current_principal` (SYS_OPEN's by-name branch, the O_CREAT owner persist) and the `sys_fgrant` grantee capture all funnel through — where a `PRIN_USER` stamp of a dead epoch is returned as `NONE`. x86's twin is `SLOT_EPOCH` beside `SLOT_USER` read by `slot_user_live`, consulted by `owned_user_ok` and `owned_user_stamp`. COST on the hot path, since SYS_OPEN is one and no panic path may be added: for a caller that is not user-stamped (the whole fixture battery; every program on a boot with no session) ONE `cmp` on a byte already in a register, inside a lock already held — two more relaxed atomic loads for a user-stamped caller, and on x86 literally zero on the admit path because `owned_user_ok` runs only on a live-incarnation DENY. No allocation, no new lock, no fallible call. GATE: `session_epoch_fixture` on both arches (`loginst`), which proves the REFUSAL and not merely the admission — a second slot of the SAME session reaches the file by name (`same_session_ok`, the control), Log Out refuses it (`refused_while_closed`), the SAME USER logs straight back in so the principal string is byte-identical and only the epoch separates the sessions and it is STILL refused and now reads ANONYMOUS (`stale_refused`, `stale_kind=0`), and a slot launched in the new session opens the same file (`fresh_ok`, so the gate is not a constant-deny). | SO55 | **THE xHCI ENUMERATOR READ 64 BYTES OF EVERY CONFIGURATION DESCRIPTOR AND WALKED wTotalLength.** `request_configuration_descriptor` (`drivers/xhci/mod.rs`) issued GET_DESCRIPTOR(CONFIGURATION, index 0) with wLength 64 and a 64-byte data TRB into the slot's 256-byte descriptor buffer, then the walk trusted `wTotalLength` up to 256: a configuration longer than 64 bytes was silently cut at its first 64 (the walk read stale zeros, hit `bLength == 0`, and stopped). FIX: wLength 0x0100, data TRB 256, cache clean 256 — the buffer's own size, and the walk's own bound. Knob-off bytes change, deliberately (a fix, not a knob). HAZARD NOTED, NOT FIXED: the same dispatch chain tests `desc_data[4]` as a device class on the CONFIGURATION descriptor's completion too, where byte 4 is bNumInterfaces — a configuration with 8 or 9 interfaces would be routed as storage or a hub. USBNET's own arm guards on the descriptor type; the stock arms do not. | every board (shared xHCI) | shared-gate | x86 QEMU `test` (usb-storage, usb-kbd, usb-tablet, usb-hub enumerate) | fixed-unflown — branch `claude/optimistic-ramanujan-r3qyu5` (the branch tip is the sha; the seat fills it at the fold) | `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp-0924/usbnet/USBNET.md` |
| SO56 | **USBNET — A USB ETHERNET LINK ON THE SHARED xHCI PATH, WITH THE CDC-ECM FRONT-END, AND THE WHOLE x86 smoltcp SURFACE RUNNING OVER IT WITH NO STACK EDIT.** rmbp-ledger B8 asked for a wired NIC and named the wrong part (the rMBP's only class-0x02 PCI device is the Broadcom Wi-Fi; the board has no wired port); what every board can carry is a USB dongle, so the job is ONE link driver on the shared bus. `drivers/xhci/usbnet.rs` (cfg-erased module, feature `usbnet`, knob `UNAOS_USBNET=1`): frame rings both ways, the one-outstanding bulk-IN arm/claim protocol (`ftdirx`'s shape), synchronous bulk-OUT TDs with a chained zero-length TRB on MPS-multiple frames, and ECM bring-up (SET_CONFIGURATION of the ECM configuration, SET_INTERFACE data alt 1, the station address from the iMACAddress STRING descriptor, SetEthernetPacketFilter). RNDIS-first devices (QEMU's `usb-net`: RNDIS index 0, ECM index 1) are handled by asking for configuration index 1 with the full length. Controller half at the file tail of xhci/mod.rs (`service_usbnet`, `usbnet_after_walk`, `usbnet_request_config`); nine same-line cfg-gated hooks in the enumeration, completion routing, disconnect paths and the event-ring claim. x86: `e1000::raw_rx/raw_tx/hw_addr` fall back to the link when no e1000 is present, so DHCP, ping, arp, dns and the ring-3 socket family run over the dongle unchanged; aarch64: `NicOps` registered into `net_phy::net6` at link-up (compiles; unflown). MEASURED (QEMU 8.2.2, dongle-only leg `UNAOS_USBNET=1 UNAOS_NOE1000=1`): `:: USBNET: up … mac=40:54:00:12:34:57 filter=ok -> PASS ::`, `SOCK-5 lease 10.0.2.30/24` (the link's own netdev range), `SOCK-1 4/4`, `SOCK-2 udp dns OK`, ring-3 `SOCK-2 -> PASS`, `SOCK-4 -> PASS`; x86-usbnet.spec 9/9; go-red by mutation. TWO DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED ON THE WAY: SO55 (the 64-byte read), and the first run's `SOCK-5 no offer / SOCK-1 0/4 / tx_drop=45322` — smolnet's pumps spin on `raw_rx` with the STACK lock held assuming a directly readable RX ring, so the link's accessors now take the controller loan themselves when it is free and move frames before answering (`usbnet::drive`). THE AX88179 FRONT-END (Peter's two `0b95:1790` dongles; `0b95:178a` too) IS BUILT ON THE SAME LINK, UNFLOWN: vendor-specific interface taken as the data interface, register access over vendor requests 0x01 (register space) / 0x02 (PHY), bring-up in the part's order (PHYPWR reset → IPRL, CLK_SELECT, NODE_ID → MAC, bulk-in aggregation by bus speed, pause levels, no checksum offload, RX_CTL, MEDIUM mode, BMCR autoneg restart), the RX trailer parsed (`pkt_cnt`/`hdr_off`, per-packet length + CRC/DROP flags, 2-byte alignment pad), the 8-byte TX header with the pad flag in place of a ZLP (`usbnet::ax`, `usbnet_bringup_ax`, `deliver_ax`). The register map is the part's as ASIX documents it and as the Linux `ax88179_178a` driver (GPL, this tree's licence) uses it; QEMU has no model, so every constant is confirmed on the bench — the wire says `kind=ax88179` and prints the MEDIUM readback and link status so a first boot reads as data, not as ECM. OWED: that bench boot (rMBP flight with a dongle; a Pi or Orin with one flies the aarch64 registration at the same time); ring-3 SOCK-3 over the link (`done=0` within the 120 s wall in the container, where the connect is refused anyway). | every board (shared xHCI; x86 smolnet seam; aarch64 net6 registry) | rmbp (USBNET; shared files: `drivers/xhci/usbnet.rs`, hooks in xhci/mod.rs and e1000.rs, arroyo, builder, Cargo) | x86 QEMU `usb-net`; then the rMBP, the Pi and the Orin with the AX88179 (owed rung) | fixed-unflown — branch `claude/optimistic-ramanujan-r3qyu5` (the branch tip is the sha; the seat fills it at the fold) | `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp-0924/usbnet/USBNET.md`; `docs/dev/OS/06_NETWORK_STACK/network_stack.md` §10 |

| SO45 | **THE 192-BYTE CAP IS ON THE PATH THE JETSON TAKES, SO 361 ms IS NOT 192 BYTES OF UART — SO29'S MECHANISM, NOT SERDRAIN'S FIX, IS WHAT METAL DISAGREES WITH.** SO31 capped one drain at `DRAIN_BYTE_BUDGET` = 192 B and predicted the drag-stall band would fall 377.8 ms -> 22.6 ms; render14 read `[comp2] max_us` = 361 130 / 359 500 / 348 593 us on boots 1/2/5 and 367 166 us on boot 3, the same band. DERIVED BY READING THE PATH, twelve hops, no `cfg` on any of them: `video/wm.rs:14451` `comp2_emit` -> `wm.rs:13462` `serial_println!` -> `arch/aarch64/serial.rs:284` macro -> `:177` `_print` -> `:203` `without_interrupts` -> `:224` `SERIAL_PORT.try_lock()` -> **`:238` `serial_ring::drain_capped`** -> `serial_ring.rs:709/710` -> `:251` `DRAIN_BYTE_BUDGET` (ungated, both arches, every image) -> `:842` `paid < 192` -> `arch/aarch64/serial.rs:141` `write_str` -> `:64` `tegra::write_byte`. `DRAINCAP_PAD` (`serial_ring.rs:1839`) is a DIFFERENT object and has never executed on this board: `witness`-gated fixture padding read only by the four selftests, all reached only from `mirror_service`, which SO41 proved is unreachable on the flight image. THE ARITHMETIC. 115200 8N1 = 11 520 B/s = 86.805 us/B. `drain_into` tests the budget BEFORE each line and strictly `<`, so one capped drain pays at most `DRAIN_BYTE_BUDGET - 1 + widest line`: 191+68 = 259 B = **22.5 ms** (SO31's shape), 191+143 = 334 B = 29.0 ms (render14 boot 1 mean line), 191+1135 = 1 326 B = 115.1 ms (that boot's widest line). `max_us` = 361 130 us / 86.805 = **4 161 B** — 21.7 budgets, 1.05 whole staging rings. ONE CAPPED DRAIN CANNOT PRODUCE IT. Two flight readings point the same way and neither is conclusive alone: render14 boot 1 has ZERO `[serial] dropped` lines where pre-SERDRAIN render13 boot 1 had `dropped 5331 lines in 192 events`, and render14 boot 3's `[pwrshutoff] ring drained lines=1 bytes=163` says the WHOLE ring held one line at shutdown on the boot that read `max_us=367166`. DELIVERABLE 2, SERWIRE: `drain_capped` — the one spelling both arches' `_print` use — charges a cycles-and-bytes odometer, and `comp2_emit` drains it on the SAME rollup and the SAME span as `max_cyc`. **A span total is an upper bound on any single pass inside it**, so `drain_us < max_us` is arithmetic proof that the pass did not spend it in the drain, with no per-pass bracket anywhere in the compositor. Latched one-shot above `SERWIRE_ARM_US` = 100 000 us. The uncapped spellings (`drain`, `power_drain`, `discard_staged`) are deliberately NOT charged — panic and power contexts are not composite passes | all (`serial_ring.rs` odometer; `video/wm.rs` `[comp2]` rollup emit) | orin | Orin bench (no aarch64 QEMU verb composites); the x86 `UNAOS_WC=1 ./arroyo test` ladder scores the fixture | fixed-unflown — the derivation is done and the instrument is in the tree with `serial_ring::serwire_selftest` green on the x86 witness ladder, DRAINCAP / SERWIT-1 / SERWIT-1B / PWRDRAIN / S5DRAIN unmoved beside it. UNFLOWN is the verdict itself: only an Orin boot can read `[serwire] … -> NOT-DRAIN` or `-> DRAIN-BOUND`. COSTS THE FLIGHT BOOT ONE LINE, bounded at 324 B (113 B literal + 10 fields x 20 digits + an 11 B verdict) and 163 B on render14's numbers; `[comp2]` is not widened by one byte (a per-rollup field would be SO30 one layer up). The fixture costs the Jetson nothing, and that is SO41's property rather than a claim about the fixture **SERIALTX (rmbp B154, 2026-09-22) EXTENDS THIS INSTRUMENT'S ANSWER AND NARROWS ITS SCOPE.** This row's question was `is [comp2] max_us the serial drain?` and its odometer is charged by `drain_capped` and nothing else, which is correct and is now known to be PARTIAL: the drain is one of four terms a `_print` pays, and the other three (own-line emission, contended spin, the four post-mask taps) had no instrument at all. `[sertx]` measures all four; `[sertx] drain_us` and `[serwire] drain_us` measure the same drains from opposite ends and must agree. AND THE rMBP READING IS THE ONE THIS ROW COULD NOT HAVE PREDICTED: that board has NO 16550 (`uart16550=absent carrier=ftdi-mirror law=emitted==0`, flight 11 `[244ms]`), so on it `drain_capped` is never reached at all, nothing is ever staged (`:: DRAINCAP: SKIP` at `[28280ms]`) and every `[comp2] wcd_us=` on the flight reads 0. A UART-drain instrument therefore reads a TRUE zero there for a configuration reason, and the console can still be the cost — via the POST-MASK taps, which is why B154 reports `taps_us`/`taps_us_max` beside the masked terms rather than folding them in. SO45's `-> NOT-DRAIN` on an Orin capture keeps its meaning exactly; what B154 adds is that `NOT-DRAIN` never meant `NOT-CONSOLE`. | SO47 | **`arp` and `dns` do not work while `ping` does, and they are TWO SEPARATE DEFECTS IN THE TWO VERBS — neither in the stack under them.** Render14 boots 4 and 5, one boot, one interface, in order: `:: NET6: ping 10.42.0.1 4/4 replies over rtl8168 peer 9c:69:d3:28:6e:f4 -> REPLY ::` (ping works AND learns the peer MAC), then `:: NET6: arp 10.42.0.1 -> NO REPLY ::` for that same address, then `:: [midden] cmd="dns google.com" -> TerminalError len=44 ::` with no `:: NET6:` line at all. **`arp`: the verb never had a neighbour table to read.** smoltcp 0.13.1 keeps its neighbour cache as a PRIVATE field of `InterfaceInner` (`neighbor_cache`, `src/iface/interface/mod.rs:134`) and publishes no reader (`NeighborCache::lookup` is `pub(crate)`), so a verb cannot ask smoltcp what it already resolved; every NET6 verb built a THROWAWAY `Interface` with an EMPTY cache and `arp`'s only source of truth was one ARP reply captured inside its own 2 s budget. That is fine on a clean wire and not on this NIC — MEASURED both ways: with the verb UNCHANGED the `virt` leg prints `:: NET6: arp 10.0.2.2 -> is-at 52:55:0a:00:02:02 ::` right after a 4/4 ping, while render14 boot4 scores six `[net5T] … verdict=NOWHERE — the payload never reached this DRAM at all` in twelve pops, several 60-byte i.e. ARP-reply-sized (A64, this ledger). Ping survives that because it needs one of four echo replies. The other two candidate shapes are REFUTED, not set aside: the verb polls the NIC ring directly (no queue nothing feeds), and its budget is not short — ping's first reply that boot took 524 ms against the same `VERB_BUDGET_MS = 2000`, and `[gui] app-enter t=118s` / `app-exit t=120s` shows `arp` burning the full two seconds. ⚠ And the tempting third reading is REFUSED: the absence of `[net4F]`/`[net5T]` lines during the `arp` window is NOT evidence of a dead wire — both witnesses are CAPPED (8 and 12 pops that boot) and were exhausted during the ping (LAWS §5). **`dns`: the verb is never reached.** `len=44` is exactly `"Unknown command. Type 'help' for assistance."` — the `Plan::Say(TerminalError)` fallthrough at `libs/sys/midden_core/src/lib.rs:512`. Since MIDDEN-M1 there is ONE command table and it is midden_core's `HOST_VERBS`, whose `// network` group registers `ifconfig`, `ping`, `arp`, `nc`, `curl` (`lib.rs:274-275`) and NOT `dns`, so the `"dns" => net6_shell_dns(…)` arm at `shell.rs:5594` has never been reachable on any build — which is why a bare `dns` with no arguments returns the same 44-byte error as `dns google.com` | all aarch64 (`crates/kernel/src/net_phy.rs` `mod net6`); the `dns` half is `libs/sys/midden_core` | orin | QEMU `virt` for the verbs; Orin bench for the metal wire | fixed-unflown — **NETVERB (this commit).** `arp` FIXED: a NET6-owned neighbour table in `mod net6` learns every ARP frame any phy in the module receives (BOTH opcodes — a request carries the sender's IP and MAC in the same fields a reply does), the persistent stack's phy now carries a `Learn` observer so the table stays warm between verbs, and `arp` reads the table first and probes only on a miss — which is also what `arp <ip>` means everywhere else (R26: it is the neighbour table, not a ping). ` -> is-at ` is held byte-for-byte; `via=cache` / `via=wire` and `age_ms=` are appended after the MAC, and the failure line carries `learned=` as a control (0 = the learn path never ran, a different defect from "this address is unknown"). `dns` HALF-FIXED, and the missing half is named rather than made: the verb now has ONE emission point every arm reaches (printing-free `dns_lookup` -> `DnsVerdict` -> one `serial_println!`), so "witnesses every path, naming the resolver and the reason" is a property of the shape; `BIND FAILED` is split out of a `SEND FAILED` that conflated two failures; a `DNS_WITNESS` counter is bumped there and the fixture asserts it moves by EXACTLY one across a lookup that returns no address. **The registration LANDED under a seat grant (2026-09-13): `("dns", Avail::Always)` in the `// network` group of `libs/sys/midden_core/src/lib.rs`, so the verb is reachable from the shell for the first time.** `Avail::Always` and NOT `Avail::Aarch64`, and that is settled by a GATE rather than by taste: `no_verb_is_pinned_to_a_platform_without_a_capability` (`lib.rs:969-976`) asserts `!matches!(a, Avail::Aarch64)` over every `HOST_VERBS` member, so the variant is empty BY TEST — registering `dns` there failed 2 of 16 (`lib.rs:972`, `lib.rs:940`). It would not have been narrower either (the arm is `net6` AND `aarch64`; the variant is only `aarch64`, so both spellings reach the drift net on an aarch64 build with `net6` off), and on the x86 half it does not make the verb absent — it makes it a typo, answering with the very 44-byte `TerminalError` this row exists to delete. R26 clause 3 is the precedent (`burst`/`simmer` moved to `Always` "for no hardware reason", the ring arm refusing honestly by name). EXACT (`Avail::Net6` + `Facts::net6`) needs a producer in `midden_facts()` (`shell.rs`) and is worse than today without one. Enforced going forward: `dns` joins the R26-clause-3 assertion list at `lib.rs:880`, and `cargo test -p midden_core` is 16/16. **THE THREE OWED ITEMS, TWO PAID (WIREHYG, orin-ledger A83/A84, 2026-09-13):** (1) `shell.rs:6103-6122` called the `other =>` drift net "unreachable by construction ... that set is empty today", which this registration makes FALSE — typing `dns` on a build without `all(net6, aarch64)` reaches it and prints an honest by-name refusal; the behaviour is right and only the comment overstated. **FIXED**, comment only and LINE-NEUTRAL (20 lines in, 20 out). (3) `unaos/arroyo:2054-2056` documents the same superseded shapes in the `net6` knob block — **STILL OWED**, two other executors held that file; the replacement text is in the WIREHYG report. | SO44 | **A CLICK ON THE LOGIN ROW STRANDS IT — the screen is not MODAL, and LOGINCLOSE's measurement was right about the close box and wrong about the consequence.** Peter, render14: *"the login window appeared over the top of the gui and when i click it it went away"*. The row is minted at `owner_asid = 0` (`video/login.rs` `OWNER`), the band `wm::hit_test` skips (`video/wm.rs:3011`) — which is what makes it unclosable (SO36) and ALSO what makes a press on it belong to somebody else. A press that reaches no row does not stop: it lands BENEATH, both routers then RAISE and FOCUS that row (`arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` `wc_click_route_at`, `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs` `wc_click_route`, each after `hit_test` names an id), and the screen is left behind the desktop it no longer covers. LOGINCLOSE's fixture asked *can the pointer close this row* and never asked *what does a press on this row DO*. MEASURED on the x86 WC ladder by the new probe, on the REAL row the CLOSE leg mints: `[login] press-probe win=1 centre=(640,331) hit=0 verdict=NOBODY` — at its own centre the screen's row is not named, so the press is not the screen's; on a boot with desktop rows under it the same read names the row beneath. The coordinates `press_swallow` had always TAKEN AND THROWN AWAY (`let _ = (x, y);`) are now USED: `login::local_of` maps the panel point through the row's own `wm` geometry — origin AND integer upscale, with the subtraction's sign checked BEFORE the divide, because Rust truncates toward zero and `-1 / 2` would put a press one pixel above the window inside its first row — and `login::ctl_at` names the control. The two fields FOCUS, the BUTTON submits (the same `submit` Enter calls, so the two ways in cannot drift), and a USER ROW picks that name out of the store (`users::name_at`, new, tail-appended to `fs/users.rs` under `#[cfg(feature = "login")]` because that module is declared unconditionally and a mid-file insert would move every panic `Location` below it). The screen therefore SHOWS who lives on this machine and carries a button, because Enter is not discoverable. ⛔ **THE RETURN VALUE DID NOT CHANGE AND MUST NOT:** still `true` for every press, hit or miss — the modality is the ROUTER's contract and is not conditioned on there being a control under the point. ⛔ **NEITHER ROUTER WAS EDITED, and that is a finding rather than an omission:** the brief allowed both at the login seam, and the seam was measured already correct at this branch's base — SESSGATE's statement is the first thing each press edge does, and `press_swallow` is reached THROUGH it. The REJECTED alternative stays rejected and is now FORBIDden by name in a spec: `wm::hit_test` naming an `owner_asid == 0` row would hand the screen a close box back (LOGINCLOSE's measured defect) and would gate only the points inside the rectangle, so `[login] press-probe … verdict=MODAL` is a FORBID and `FALLS-THROUGH` is the EXPECTED reading of that probe — the standing proof of why modality has to live at the router. Its control term is `outside_quiet`: a press at `(0,0)` must be CONSUMED and must answer NO control, so a `ctl_at` that said yes to every point fails exactly one term and that term is this one. GO-RED: delete the session-gate statement from `wc_click_route_at` and the press falls to the window arm, which raises the row beneath; the router still answers `true` (it consumed the press FOR THAT ROW) so `*_press` stays true, and `pw_focus`/`nm_focus`/`row_picked`/`opened` all go false. Pinned by `unaos/scripts/specs/x86-login.spec` §4 — the first spec in this repo to read ANY of the LOGIN arc's five fixtures; `grep -rn LOGIN scripts/specs/` was empty before it. | SO46 | **`[comp2] blit_us` IS NOT A BLIT RATE — IT IS THE WHOLE PER-WINDOW LOOP, AND FOUR WITNESS-ONLY SITES RUN INSIDE IT.** SO45 continued: §SERWIRE settled by arithmetic that one capped drain cannot produce `max_us=361130` (4 161 B = 1.05 whole rings), leaving the lead that the stall rollup's blit moves 3 053 700 B at **32 B/us** where the same boot's healthy rollups sustain **109**. Those are not the same measurement. `comp2_emit` prints `blit_us` as `loop_cyc - cache_cyc`, and `C2_LOOP_CYC` (`video/wm.rs:6413`) is charged `now_cycles() - c2_loop0` at the close of the WHOLE per-window loop. Between the open and that charge sit five sites that move no pixel and exist only because the image carries `witness`: `wcg::end` (`wm.rs:6362`, a scan-out READ-BACK plus two `[wc-g]` lines), `wcg::stage_flush` (`:6378`, `[wc-h]`), `band_flush` (`:6382`, `[wc-b]`), `chromeband_fixture` (`:6386`, x86) and `verify_window` (`:6398`, a second read-back plus `[wc-d]`) — and only the last has a field of its own (`wcd_us`). So `bytes_pp / blit_us` is a copy rate ONLY on a span where none of them fired, and the loop's own comment at the charge ("never [perturb] the rollup average this line is read for") is FALSE on exactly the rollup SO45 is about, because that rollup is the boot's FIRST and every per-window-id one-shot is still unfired. **THE PARTITION IS PERFECT** over render14 boot 1's 38 rollups (`awk 'index($0,"[comp2] rollup")'` on the evidence log, counting `[wc-g] win=` lines between rollups): the six rollups carrying a WC-G sample or `wcd_us>0` are r1 r2 r3 r4 r33 r38, and **r1..r4 are the four slowest of the boot at 32.2 / 40.2 / 51.8 / 71.7 B/us** while the **32 witness-free rollups sit at 68.7..163.0, mean 109**. No rollup with a read-back is in the healthy band and none without one is in the stall band; the recovery is monotonic as the one-shots retire. **IT REPRODUCES:** boot 3 r1 reads `passes=4 max_us=361880 blit_us=94910 wcd_us=41937` with `bytes_pp` at 3 053 700 B against boot 1's `passes=4 max_us=361130 blit_us=94713 wcd_us=41944` with `bytes_pp` at 3 053 700 B — the same 32.2 B/us on a different boot, which contention and a mapping fault do not do and a boot-phase structure does. Boot 3's other peak (`max_us=367166`, r5) also carries three WC-G samples. **THE ARITHMETIC:** r1's bracket total is `4 x 94 713` = 378 852 us and `max_us` is **95.3 %** of it. Charged inside it and measured on the same wire: `[wc-g] prof win=1 seq=0/seq=1` four-phase sums (`cks_blit + civac + cks_after + readback`) = 27 640 + 27 396 = **55 036 us**, and `[comp2] wcd_us=` = **41 944 us** — **96 980 us = 25.6 % of the bracket and 26.9 % of `max_us`, with not one byte of it a pixel on glass.** `[wc-g] rollup win=1 wit_us=109867` over all four of win=1's samples (`wcg.rs:387`/`:3901`) is the independent check. It is a LOWER bound: it excludes the span's 1 686 B of in-loop `[wc-g]`/`[wc-h]`/`[wc-b]`/`[wc-d]`, up to 146 353 us more at 86.805 us/B. **WHAT IS NOT PROVEN, AND IS WHY THIS SHIPS AS AN INSTRUMENT AND NOT A FIX:** with the MEASURED terms subtracted the remainder is 281 872 us = **43 B/us**, still 2.5x under the healthy 109; with the whole bracket modelled (UART at its ceiling) it is 135 519 us = **90 B/us**, inside the band. The wire cannot separate those two because nothing charges the print sites, and every surviving candidate (destination mapping attributes, an alignment/width fallback, memory-port contention with scan-out) lives on the far side of that gap. **BLITWIRE** closes it: `C2_WIT_CYC` brackets the witness-only region (opened on `comp_mark(rw.id, 4)` at `wm.rs:6361`, closed on `wcn_note_drawn` at `:6406`; straight-line, no `continue`/`break`/`return`/`?` between them), charged on EVERY drawn window because a counter charged only when it is large cannot be read as a share, with `C2_WIT_N` so the total is never misread as a per-pass mean, drained in `comp2_emit`'s existing sweep on every rollup so the odometer stays a SPAN. One LATCHED line, at most once per boot, bounded at **345 B** (112 B literal + 11 fields x 20 digits + a 13 B verdict), 166 B on the render14 numbers; `[comp2]` is not widened by one byte and there is NO per-rollup field — at ~140 rollups on a 700 s boot that is SO30 one layer up. Hot path: two `now_cycles()` and two relaxed atomics per drawn window, witness-only, zero UART | all (`video/wm.rs`; the defect is in what the instrument MEASURES, not in either arch's transport) | orin | x86 `UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 UNAOS_WC=1 ./arroyo test 90` (BLITWIRE fixture); `UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test-arm`; `UNAOS_TEGRA=1 ./arroyo esp-jetson` + `UNAOS_WITNESS=1 UNAOS_TEGRA=1 ./arroyo esp-jetson` with `LC_ALL=C grep -a -o -F` on both built ELFs; the Orin bench scores the line | open — diagnosed-unflown: the PARTITION and the 25.6 % are measured off two flight captures and are facts about this boot pair; the WITNESS-BOUND / COPY-BOUND verdict is the PREDICTION and only metal can settle it. `blitwire_selftest` drives `blitwire_calc` and `blitwire_arm` — the same objects the wire calls — with r1's own numbers, and its row A is the realistic regression (charge the bracket, forget to subtract it: `net_bus = raw_bus = 32 -> COPY-BOUND`, sending the next arc after a mapping bug that is not there). The `BLITWIRE_ARM_US` / `BLITWIRE_FLOOR_BUS` rows are pinned by ungated `const _: () = assert!(...)`, for SO40's stated reason. ⚠ THE SERIAL-RING QUESTION IS NOT REOPENED HERE and nothing in `serial_ring.rs` or either `arch/*/serial.rs` is touched: §SERWIRE's arithmetic stands, and this row is the other half of the same adjudication — a boot printing `-> NOT-DRAIN` and `-> WITNESS-BOUND` has closed SO45 from both sides [ST221] | `docs/dev/OS/08_VIDEO/composite_blit_bracket.md` §BLITWIRE; `video/wm.rs` (`C2_WIT_CYC`, `C2_WIT_N`, `BLITWIRE_ARM_US`, `BLITWIRE_FLOOR_BUS`, `blitwire_calc`, `blitwire_arm`, `blitwire_note`, `blitwire_selftest`); `docs/dev/OS/02_KERNEL_CORE/serial_transport.md` §SERWIRE; LEDGER SO29/SO30/SO31/SO45; `docs/dev/evidence/orin28/render14-boot1-desktop-menubar.log` + `render14-boot3-shutdown-rung4e.log` |
| SR1 | **A `UNAOS_*` knob with no `K8_FEATS` arm is UNREACHABLE for every Pi bare-metal image, silently.** `kernel8()` builds from a CURATED list (`arroyo`, `local K8_FEATS="baremetal,skip_xhci"`) that deliberately does not draw from the general `_feats` map — 113 distinct knobs there, 42 arms here — so an omitted arm produces no error and no warning. **NOT the same defect as S9/KNOBLEG**, whose invariant is that every aarch64-qualified feature is COMPILED by some check leg: a knob can be fully leg-covered and still unreachable in the flashed image an operator boots. Build coverage vs operator reachability. Two instances, a week apart, different seats — which is what makes it a class: `UNAOS_PRTSCRST` (pi 7; the Print Screen gate greened about nothing and blocked S14 for a week) and `UNAOS_BOOTLOG` (orin 15 via CONSOLEQUIET; since `3329eec6` a `UNAOS_PIDESK=1` image has no way to restore the serial→panel mirror). **Both arms now added** (`arroyo`, beside the `UNAOS_LOGTS` arm whose own comment already documented the trap); default-off, so an unset environment leaves `K8_FEATS` byte-identical. **The CLASS is still open.** Most of the 113-vs-42 gap is correct — `KEPLER*`/`IVB*`/`XCARVE*`/`WIFI*` are x86-only, `ORIN*`/`TEGRA*` are the Orin's — and by inspection a Pi-meaningful knob that was never given an arm is indistinguishable from one deliberately omitted (pi 7's point, and it is the right one). Closing the class needs a gate that asserts every Pi-eligible knob has an arm OR carries an explicit x86/Orin-only allowlist, making an omission a recorded decision. Not started | all (Pi images) | rmbp (`arroyo` is a gate file) | Pi bench | open — both instances fixed; the CLASS is now MEASURED and GATED by **GATE-K8REACH** (rmbp 14, 2026-09-06, rmbp-ledger B28): 115 knobs in `_feats`, 11 armed, 104 registered unarmed — silent entry is closed, a knob added tomorrow reds until someone rules on it. What remains open is the BACKLOG, not the hole: the 104 seeded `TODO` rows (65 with at least one Pi-live cfg site, 39 with none), each converted to an arm or to `NA <reason>` by the seat that owns the knob. The gate deliberately does not rule on Pi-meaningfulness itself — pi 7's point holds and `scripts/k8-reach.py --evidence <KNOB>` is what makes a ruling cheap | pi 7's measurement at `dc811eee` (their SP1, dropped in favour of this row); orin 15's `CONSOLEQUIET.md` §4; `unaos/arroyo` K8_FEATS block |
| SR2 | **Print Screen wedges the machine for the whole capture, on every board — the write happens inside the device-service pass.** Promoted from `rmbp-ledger` A2, which measured it on the 2012 rMBP: **70 s per capture** for a 15.5 MB PNG at ~220 KB/s with `[deadman] pmp=0` for the entire write — keyboard and mouse dead throughout. **Instance 2, the Orin (2026-09-06, Peter at the glass, render7 boot 1): "Print Screen wedges the machine for seconds; 3 fast presses did not yield 3 captures"**, alongside orin's A17 gap of 8 armed / 7 OK (SCREEN0..6) / 0 refusals / **1 silent**. Two boards, two architectures, one shape — the capture is synchronous inside the pass that also services input, so the machine is unresponsive for its duration and presses arriving during it are lost rather than queued. **This is why it is a shared row and not two arch rows**: the fix is an incremental or async capture in shared code, not a per-board workaround, and A17's "silent" capture is what a dropped press looks like from the other end | all | rmbp (`video/prtscr.rs`, the capture path) | rmbp + orin benches | flown — PRTSCR3 (the sliced capture, `Job::begin()` on `mount_capture_target()`, prtscr.rs:1082 at the tip) FLEW PASS on render8 2026-09-06 (orin-ledger A36: `prtscr3: armed=5 capturing=4 ok=4 slices=32 … inflight_refusals=1`); the machine is no longer wedged for a capture. A36 stays open for the SECOND, different loss that flight found (owner rmbp) — this row's own claim is closed. | SR3 | **quarry's cache-invalidation stamp advances only for USB arrivals, so a CARD (re)registration never invalidates it — and its doc comment claims the opposite.** `video/quarry/live.rs:456-458` defines `volume_gen()` as `block::usb_publish_gen()` under `cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")`, and `Model::collect_cached` (`:754`) clears the cache only when that value moves. Measured in this tree: `USB_PUBLISH_GEN.fetch_add` has exactly two sites, both inside `publish_usb_geometry` (`drivers/block.rs:694`, `:710`), reached from `xhci/mod.rs:11758` — arch-neutral, so a STICK arrival does advance it on aarch64. `register_sd` (the Pi) and `register_tegra_sd` (the Orin) advance it ZERO times, so no card event ever invalidates the cache. **The comment at `:445-453` asserts the inverse where it matters most** — "not FAT-specific and not namespace-specific … it will mean the same thing when ORIN's UnaFS is the backend" — which is false precisely for the Orin, whose card never passes through `publish_usb_geometry`; `quarry.md:542` and `:707` repeat it in prose. **CORRECTED 2026-09-06 by orin 18's push-back, and the first version's REASON was this seat's error, not just its conclusion:** the row said x86 was consistent rather than broken because `mount_prefixes()` is empty there, so there was nothing to invalidate. That conflates two different objects — the MOUNT LIST with the LISTING CACHE. `collect_cached` (`:753`) is not gated on `self.mounts` at all; it is called from `landing` (`:816`), `expand` (`:861`) and `show` (`:918`), none arch-gated, and on x86 `volume_gen()` is a literal `0`, so **if that path runs at all the cache never invalidates for the life of the model.** What remains genuinely UNDETERMINED, and is not settleable by reading: whether quarry's tree can be non-empty on x86 when `mount_prefixes()` returns empty, since `reload_roots` seeds the tree only from that list. **The check that settles it is a run, not a grep** — open quarry on x86 with `UNAOS_QUARRY=1` and read whether the tree has rows and whether `hits` ever increments. **Second defect, same counter, all boards: `unpublish_usb_geometry` never advances the generation.** Verified here — the whole function body at `drivers/block.rs` contains zero `fetch_add`; it LOADS the counter only to compare against `captured_gen`. That FALSIFIES the contract written at `video/quarry/live.rs:448` ("advanced by every geometry publish AND EVERY RETRACTION"), so a stick REMOVAL invalidates nothing and quarry serves a listing of a volume that is gone until the next arrival bumps it. **Note what is NOT exposed and why: SR2's prtscr guard asks TWO facts — `usb_info().is_some()` AND the generation — so the presence half catches exactly this case. The same counter is safe under a two-fact consumer and unsafe under a one-fact consumer**, which is the argument for fixing the counter rather than adding a second fact to quarry. Both raised by orin 18's registry pass as measured-by-executor and re-derived here before being written down. **Found by orin 18 while grepping their own branch for text to fix under B46 — the fourth stale statement this week found by someone reading for a different purpose, and the third guard-that-cannot-fire found today** (rmbp's name-based `same_volume` → `rmbp-ledger` B60, prtscr's USB-scoped probe → SR2, this). Two honest fix shapes, unchosen: a generic block epoch every registrar bumps, or an explicit statement that the stamp is USB-only plus a second event for card registration. Two of quarry's three invalidation events still work, so this is a stale-cache hole, not a dead cache. **FIXED 2026-09-15 (NSGEN, `exec-rmbp-vidsmall`, parent `31c7e5cb`), and NEITHER of the row's two unchosen fix shapes was taken — a THIRD one was, because the row's own diagnosis names the wrong layer.** A "generic block epoch every registrar bumps" still cannot see a `mkdir`: `drivers/block.rs` never learns that `create_dir` ran, so a block-layer counter would fix the CARD half and leave the whole namespace half — the half RMDIR/SO18 had just made reachable — exactly as broken. The event Quarry needs is a NAMESPACE event. So: `fs::NS_GEN` (`fs/mod.rs`, an arch-neutral `AtomicU64` in `fs/`, never in a driver) is advanced by `fs::ns_bump` wrapped around the resolve-then-dispatch line of all six `MountTable` mutators — `create` (`fs/vfs.rs:562`), `write` (`:567`), `truncate` (`:572`), `unlink` (`:577`), `rename` (`:708`), `remove_dir` (`:743`) — ABOVE the backend dispatch, so every backend inherits it and no backend implements anything. All six are LINE-NEUTRAL one-for-one replacements (`git diff -U0` on that file is six `@@ -N +N @@` hunks plus one file-tail append), which `fs/vfs.rs` owes because it is lexed into the knob-off `kernel8.img` and panic `Location`s embed line numbers. It bumps on `Ok` only: a `mkdir` onto an existing name, a write to a read-only volume and a cross-volume `mv` change no listing, and a counter that moved for them would churn every consumer's cache on the operations that changed nothing. `volume_gen()` (`video/quarry/live.rs`) now ADDS the two monotone numbers on aarch64 — `usb_publish_gen().wrapping_add(fs::ns_gen())` — so the USB arrival the row credits still advances the stamp through the same function rather than being replaced, and reads `fs::ns_gen()` alone on x86, where the arm was the literal `0` the row's correction names. The doc comment the row convicts ("not FAT-specific and not namespace-specific") is rewritten to say what is true and to carry the SR3 history. **WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT FIX, and it is the row's own second defect: `unpublish_usb_geometry` still never advances `USB_PUBLISH_GEN`** — `drivers/block.rs` is outside this brief's file set and another executor holds it today, so a stick REMOVAL still invalidates nothing on the USB half. The namespace half now covers every mutation UnaOS itself performs; a retraction performed by a human hand is still unheard. That is a one-line `fetch_add` in `unpublish_usb_geometry` and it is owed. Wire: `:: QUARRYSTAMP: … -> PASS ::` (`video/quarry/live.rs::stamp_selftest`, `witness`), chained from BOTH family arms (`selftest` for aarch64's desktop, `door_selftest` for x86's `crystal::selftest` tail) so no router file another lane owns needed a line. | all (every board; x86 was the WORST case — a constant stamp, never a stale one) | rmbp | pi + orin benches | fixed-unflown — gate, no flight; on `exec-rmbp-vidsmall` off `31c7e5cb`, lands with the rmbp landing. The `unpublish_usb_geometry` half above is LEFT OPEN and is a `drivers/block.rs` edit | `grep -n "fn volume_gen" -A 6 unaos/crates/kernel/src/video/quarry/live.rs` and `grep -rn USB_PUBLISH_GEN unaos/crates/kernel/src/drivers/block.rs`, both run 2026-09-06 on `hw-rmbp`; orin 18's independent census over their tree agrees. FIX MEASURED 2026-09-15: `./arroyo check` rc=0 (both arches, full cfg matrix, `x86-all` carries `wc,witness,quarry,facet` so the new fixture is type-checked); ONE run `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_QUARRY=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test-fat sf 200` rc=0 carrying the QUARRYSTAMP PASS line (`UNAOS_QUARRY=1` is REQUIRED and the brief's command omitted it — `wc` does not imply `quarry`, so without it `video/quarry` is not compiled and `door_selftest` is never called); GREEN `:: QUARRYSTAMP: before=0 after=5 mutations=5 seam=fs::vfs::MountTable live_mounts=3 quiet_list=true quiet_live=true mkdir=true/true create=true/true write=true/true eexist=true denied=true quiet_refusal=true rm=true/true rmdir=true/true clean=true -> PASS ::`, artifact-certified with `LC_ALL=C grep -a -o -F QUARRYSTAMP` on the built ELF rather than off the banner; GO-RED by deleting the `ns_bump` at `MountTable::create` -> `before=0 after=3 mutations=3 … mkdir=true/false create=true/false … -> FAIL ::`, run rc=1, reverted (`git diff -U0` back to six `@@ -N +N @@`) |
| SR4 | **A mount's volume IDENTITY was its NAME, so one physical volume mounted twice under two names read as two volumes — and the transcript could not convict it because it used the router under test as its own oracle.** Found by rmbp 15 reviewing VFSROUTE (`rmbp-ledger` B60, condition C1): `MountTable::same_volume` compared `volume_name()` strings while `sdmmc_root_bind` mounts one Tegra card at `/` as `card` and `/fat` as `fat` — the inverse of the pointer-comparison bug VFSROUTE's own gate had already caught, arriving through the other door, on the ROOTFS config the boot disk is built for. **FIXED by orin 18's VOLID (`e668ebde`, patch-first on `f3e64daf`), reviewed and ACCEPTED here with the diff read rather than the report:** `fn volume_id(&self) -> Option<u64>` is REQUIRED (no default — a constant default would make every non-overriding backend one volume, the corrupting direction) and OPTIONAL (`None` = cannot establish). `same_storage` is the one comparison both `same_volume` and `rename` now call, with an `addr_eq` floor first and then `match (a, b) { (Some(x), Some(y)) => x == y, _ => false }` — **so `None` is never equal to anything including another `None`, which is the trap a bare `Option` comparison would have walked into.** FatBackend mixes a domain tag, the source name and `FatFs::volume_fingerprint()` — `(BS_VolID, count_of_clusters)`, the primitive the aarch64 ACL store already binds to — and NOT the device-wide `volume_serials` census, which this seat had objected to as a second copy of the same defect one layer down. **The oracle is fixed too, and that is the durable half:** `vfsroute.samevol` asserts the router's answer against `stat(/fat/<probe>)` matching the root listing's kind and size, a different verb consulting no identity. Failability proven on BOTH boards by falsifying `same_volume` to a constant, exit 1 each — and in the x86 red the `ls` and `cat` legs still PASS, which is the self-oracle defect demonstrating its own repair: before VOLID those legs moved WITH the bug and stayed green. **Natural experiment nobody designed:** the Pi `/fat` id differs across three image builds while `/` (UnaFS) is identical every time, because `make-pi-img.sh:288` runs `mkfs.fat` per build and seeds `BS_VolID` from the clock — the card really was reformatted and the id followed the volume. | SR13 | **GATE-LEDGER's strict trigger CANNOT FIRE IN A DETACHED WORKTREE — which is the exact shape the fleet uses to gate another seat's sha.** `ledger-check.sh:165` arms strict on `_branch == TRUNK`, and `_branch` comes from `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD`, which returns the literal string HEAD in any detached checkout **even when that checkout is at trunk's own sha**.
