Enumerated over all five live checkouts 2026-09-10: `UnaOS` on `main` ARMS; `UnaOS-rmbp`, `UnaOS-orin`, `UnaOS-hw-pi4` and a detached scratch worktree all DO NOT. Detached peers and track-branch seats are both silently unarmed and neither is told. **THE PART THAT EXPLAINS WHY IT SURVIVED THIS LONG, and it is orin 25's: THE GATE CANNOT BE VALIDATED BY THE ACTOR IT WORKS FOR.** The only seat that ever sees strict armed is the landing seat on `main`, for whom the trigger is not broken, so the one actor positioned to notice is the one with nothing to notice. **EQUALITY-NOT-ANCESTRY WAS CHECKED AGAINST THE CASE THAT COULD HAVE REFUTED IT, on this seat's own history rather than a hypothetical:** `hw-rmbp` at `a51a0396` CONTAINED trunk `751cb816` and was a different sha, for the three and a half hours between the fold `85bc4769` and the landing. Under an ancestry test every post-fold track branch in the fleet would arm strict for that whole window and red on peer rows that are legitimately branch-local — the same false-red the rejected 'zero rows of that prefix' discriminator was turned down for. Equality is the only form that survives it. **⚠ P14 CARRIES A THIRD STALE CLAUSE AND THIS ROW IS WHY — NOT EDITED HERE.** Peter named two stale sentences in P14 on 2026-09-10 and ordered them removed; a third survives on ALL THREE pushed refs, measured 2026-09-11: `origin/main`, `origin/hw-pi4` and `origin/hw-jetson` each still say the trunk enforces automatically because THE TRIGGER IS THE BRANCH, NOT AN EXPORTED VARIABLE. | SR14 | **`./arroyo test`'s VERDICT WAS NEGATIVE-ONLY, SO A RUN THAT STOPPED EARLY PASSED — and the failure mode is load-dependent, which points it the wrong way.** The orin session measured it twice on ONE tree (QUEUE §5, the 2026-09-13 RASTWIN row): idle, the rast demo took 3,553 ms, the boot reached the failing fixture and the verb exited **1**; under load the demo took 6,662 ms, the boot never got there before the wall, and the SAME command on the SAME tree exited **0**. The cause is not the wall, it is the CHECKER: `scan_serial_faults` reports the ABSENCE of fault text, and a log that stops before the fault would have been printed satisfies that perfectly. **So the gate passed BECAUSE it ran out of wall, and a loaded box — exactly what a session with several executors produces — hid reds at the moment reds were most likely.** THE FIX IS A POSITIVE CLAIM, because no quantity of negative evidence distinguishes "nothing went wrong" from "not much happened": `scripts/specs/x86-test.spec` declares ONE `COMPLETE` marker, `x86_test_completion` asks `qemu_await.py --settled` for it over the finished capture, and the verdict is three-valued — marker present hands the log to the unchanged fault scan, `truncated` / `nosignal` / a broken checker are each rc=1 with `-> TRUNCATED` on stdout and `completion=` in the `.run` sidecar. **The marker was MEASURED, not chosen from the source:** two full-wall runs at `27716175` on the rmbp-0915 bench show a default boot (1537 lines) and a `UNAOS_WC=1` boot (2149 lines, 612 more, ALL of them earlier) ending their ladder at the same zeolite block — which is why a compositor line would have false-redded every healthy default run, the mistake `x86-witness.spec`'s own SCOPE note warns about one layer up. GO-RED BY MUTATION, the wall being what is mutated: `./arroyo test 8` rc=**1** `completion=truncated`, `./arroyo test 120` rc=**0** `completion=complete complete_line=1984`; before the change the 8 s run exited 0. **THREE THINGS THIS DELIBERATELY DOES NOT DO, so nobody reads more into it:** it does not shorten any wall (→ SR7 — `test`'s wall lives in `builder/src/main.rs`'s `thread::sleep`, which this executor was not scoped to touch, so the verb still pays its full `secs` and `UNAOS_QEMU_FULL` semantics are untouched); it does not change the fault patterns or `test-arm`'s and `kernel8-test`'s verdicts; and **it does NOT close → B95, because it wires the spec by NAMING A PATH — the hard-coding shape Peter caught in that row — so it adds a sixth named-path spec invocation that B95's discovery-based fix must later replace.** `test-fat` and `test-selfhost` enter through the same function and therefore inherit the requirement; `test-selfhost` swallows the rc with its existing or-true guard (written in `arroyo` as a doubled pipe, not quoted here — a literal pipe splits a ledger cell even inside backticks, B63) and keeps its own positive witness gate | all (shared `arroyo`) | rmbp (`arroyo`, `scripts/qemu_await.py`, x86 specs) | rmbp bench (QEMU) | fixed-unflown — gate only, no metal; on `exec-rmbp-testtrunc` (parent `27716175`), the seat's fold stamps the sha. **The finding's "it does not shorten any wall" clause was true of THAT commit and is now superseded for `test`: FASTTEST (→ SR7, `exec-rmbp-fasttest`) moved the wait into `builder/src/main.rs`, so this verb exits at completion + grace. | SR15 | **A 64-BIT LBA WAS NARROWED TO 32 BITS WITH `as` AT EIGHT SITES IN THE SHARED BLOCK LAYER, AND THE ONLY THING KEEPING IT CORRECT IS THE WIDTH OF A SCSI REPLY FIELD.** `drivers/block.rs` handed `lba as u32` to the BOT pump at all eight xHCI entry points — `storage_read10` / `storage_write10` and their `_on` twins, single-block and counted. `as` on an out-of-range value does not fail, it TRUNCATES: LBA `0x1_0000_0000` becomes `0`, which is THE BOOT SECTOR — a read that returns the wrong sector as if it were right, and a write that destroys the partition table with no error anywhere. **The same defect class as orin ledger A57, at a DIFFERENT ceiling, and the difference is the whole reading of the QUEUE row that raised this.** A57's four sites truncated a BYTE OFFSET (`(lba * 512) as u32`), so their ceiling was 4 GiB of medium; READ(10)'s CDB bytes 2..5 are a SECTOR number, so these eight top out at `u32::MAX` SECTORS = 2 TiB at 512 B, 4096x further out. No byte offset is formed in `block.rs` at all — which is the invariant to keep, because the trap is narrowing before the divide. **REACHABILITY TODAY IS AN ACCIDENT, and that is the argument for a refusal rather than a comment:** the geometry bounding every one of the eight is published from `scsi_read_capacity10` (`drivers/xhci/mod.rs:11864`), whose `last_lba` is itself a `u32`, so `num_blocks <= 0x1_0000_0000` and the pre-existing `lba >= dev.num_blocks` guard keeps the argument inside 32 bits. The wrap is UNREACHABLE on this tree — guarded by the width of a reply field, not by anything this layer decides. A READ CAPACITY(16) (the command a >2 TiB device requires, and the reason `last_lba == 0xffff_ffff` is an 'ask again with the 16-byte form' sentinel) removes that guard silently and all eight wrap at once. **WHAT THE QUEUE ROW ACTUALLY POINTED AT, measured: it was ALREADY FIXED, on another track.** QUEUE §3's terse row reads "`read_block_at` `(lba*512) as u32` truncation above 4 GiB (flagged by sdv1, unledgered)"; `read_block_at` + `(lba*512) as u32` + 4 GiB is `arch/aarch64/sdmmc_tegra.rs`, and orin ledger A57 — whose own text ends "flagged earlier by sdv1 and never ledgered until now" — folded all six of that file's sites onto `sd_block_arg` and shipped the SDARG fixture (folded `ee5e2109`). So the shared-queue row was a STALE DUPLICATE of a closed track row, and this SR is the shared block layer's OWN instance of the class, found by sweeping for it. population=`grep -rn 'as u32' unaos/crates/kernel/src/` filtered to LBA / sector / byte-offset contexts across `drivers/block.rs`, `fs/bootdisk.rs`, `fs/fat.rs`, the xHCI CBW/CDB builder, `drivers/ahci.rs`, `drivers/sdhc.rs`, `drivers/emmc2.rs`, `arch/aarch64/sdmmc_tegra.rs`. **THE SWEEP'S OTHER ANSWERS, each stated so nobody re-runs it:** `fs/fat.rs` — CLEAN, and by construction: `parse_bpb` refuses a volume whose `part_lba + tot_sec` exceeds `u32::MAX + 1` (`fat.rs:1537`), so every derived LBA fits, and its many `as u32` casts are on BPB fields and FAT entries, never on a byte offset. `fs/bootdisk.rs` — CLEAN, hits=0 (its two `as u32` are a hit count and a hex nibble). `drivers/ahci.rs` — CLEAN: `read_block_at` is `u64` end to end into a 48-bit READ DMA EXT. The xHCI CBW builder — CLEAN: `(blocks as u32) * 512` (`xhci/mod.rs:11921`, `:11936`) is a BLOCK COUNT, `u16`-bounded, not an LBA. `drivers/sdhc.rs` — CLEAN (`lba_arg`, `u32::try_from` on both arms). **`drivers/emmc2.rs` WAS NOT CLEAN — and IS NOW (EMMC2LBA, 2026-09-15):** its block-addressed arm was a bare `lba as u32` at `:736` (`read_block_512`) and `:805` (`write_block_512`), missing the `lba > u32::MAX` refusal that `sd_block_arg` and `sdhc.rs::lba_arg` both carry; its byte-addressed arm was already checked (a `checked_mul(512)` bounded to `u32::MAX`). Same accidental guard as the eight above (`lba >= card.num_blocks`, CSD-derived, and an SDXC tops out at 2 TiB = 2^32 sectors), so it was unreachable for the same non-reason. Both sites now go through ONE helper at that file's tail, `card_block_arg(op, block_addressing, lba)`, written in `sd_block_arg`'s shape rather than `lba_arg`'s so a reader comparing the two aarch64 SD drivers sees one shape: the block arm refuses above `u32::MAX` SECTORS (2 TiB), the byte arm above `u32::MAX` BYTES (4 GiB, last sector 8,388,607), and a refusal prints `:: [emmc2] lba refused=0x… reason=exceeds-32-bit op=CMD17 (or CMD24) mode=block (or byte) … ::` — named, because a silent `BlockError::BadLba` here is indistinguishable from the geometry bound one line above it, and telling those two apart is the whole point of having refused instead of wrapped. Fixture `emmc2lba_selftest` (`witness`, folded line-neutrally onto `onecard_witness`'s closing line, the `fs/bootdisk.rs:1893` idiom): FOUR ceiling positives (block LBA 0 and 4,294,967,295; byte LBA 0 and 8,388,607 → `0xffff_fe00`) and FOUR negative controls (block `1 << 32` and `u64::MAX`; byte 8,388,608 — exactly 4 GiB — and `u64::MAX`, which overflows the multiply outright), with `wrapblk=0x00000000` and `wrapbyte=0x00000000` computed on the wire so the capture carries the defect rather than a claim about it. AND THE CALL-SITE GAP THIS ROW NAMED IS NOW CLOSED MECHANICALLY: the grep gate this row said "would make it mechanical and is not built" IS built — **GATE-LBA32**, `unaos/scripts/lba32-check.sh` + `scripts/lba32.registry`, one probe call in `./arroyo check` beside fc2's (`docs/dev/STRUCTURAL_GATES.md` §GATE-LBA32) | all (shared `drivers/block.rs`; `drivers/emmc2.rs` on the Pi) | rmbp | rmbp bench (QEMU x86 `test`) | fixed-unflown — gate only, no metal; on `exec-rmbp-blocksmall` (parent `5e85a7a9`), the seat's fold stamps the sha. ONE helper, `read10_lba32(op, lba)` returning `Result<u32, BlockError>`, at the FILE TAIL of `drivers/block.rs`: it refuses with a named witness and `BlockError::BadLba` — the same error the geometry bound already returns, so no caller learns a new shape — and all EIGHT sites call it, line for line in place so no `core::panic::Location` in the file moves. UNGATED: correctness in the shared block layer, no new knob. Fixture `lba32_selftest` (`witness`, chained from `fs/bootdisk.rs::unafsroot_selftest`, the one heap-up line x86, virt and the Pi all pass through) is PURE — no MMIO, no allocation, no disk — because the kernel crate is `no_std` and has no `#[cfg(test)]` idiom: two positives at the exact ceiling (LBA 0 and LBA 4,294,967,295) and THREE negative controls (`1 << 32` — the first unaddressable sector, whose old value was `0`; one past it; `u64::MAX`), so a helper that answered with a number for every input cannot pass. The wire carries the defect rather than a claim about it: `wrapto=0x00000000` is `(1u64 << 32) as u32` computed in the fixture, and `byteoff=2199023255552` is that same sector's offset in `u64` (2 TiB), printed so this row's ceiling cannot be confused with A57's 4 GiB one. Go-red MEASURED, not predicted: the helper's `u32::try_from` mutated back to the wrapping `Ok(lba as u32)` gives `refused=0/3 … -> FAIL` and run rc=1; reverted. **THE FIXTURE'S BOUND, STATED RATHER THAN OVERSOLD:** it is a known-answer test on the HELPER, so reverting one CALL SITE to `lba as u32` is INVISIBLE to it — and no behavioural leg can close that gap, because the `lba >= dev.num_blocks` geometry bound refuses an out-of-range LBA with the same `BlockError::BadLba` one layer earlier, so a wrapped and an unwrapped site are indistinguishable from any caller on any reachable input. What guards the eight sites is the TYPE (`read10_lba32` returns `Result<u32, BlockError>`, so a site that drops it does not compile into the same expression), this row, and — since 2026-09-15 — **GATE-LBA32**, which is the grep gate this row asked for. `emmc2.rs:736` / `:805` ARE NOW CLOSED (EMMC2LBA, on `exec-rmbp-smallfix4`, parent `1c7c1733`; the seat's fold stamps the sha): one helper `card_block_arg`, both sites, ungated, no new knob, with `emmc2lba_selftest`'s eight vectors executed by `UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo kernel8-test` — QEMU `raspi4b` mounts the SD through this very driver, so the run that scores the fixture is also the run that proves the mount path is unchanged (`K3-mount … byte-verified PASS` and the FAT witnesses). GATE-LBA32 is what guards the two call sites from here: its go-red is exactly the mutation no fixture in this tree can see — one site reverted to `lba as u32`, probe rc=0 → rc=1. UNFLOWN ON PI METAL: the Pi track owns that flight [ST225, ST226] | `docs/dev/OS/07_USB_STORAGE/usb_xhci.md` §36.2 for the mechanism, the two ceilings, the accidental-guard argument and the go-red; `docs/dev/OS/orin-ledger.md` A57 for the same class on the Tegra SD path — the row this one's QUEUE entry was a stale duplicate of; `docs/dev/QUEUE.md` §3 for the original `sdv1` flag; `docs/dev/STRUCTURAL_GATES.md` §GATE-LBA32 for the structural gate that now guards every call site of all three transports, with its eight controls and its two recorded go-reds |

Guarding a baseline → compare the artifact that EXCLUDES the change (identical = baseline safe). Proving a change landed → score the artifact that CONTAINS it (witness/test image), or `strings`/`grep -a -o` at the site — never `strings -n 4`, it breaks at em-dashes.** Both are needed for a gated change; neither substitutes. Same family as S17/S31 — a true check answering a different question, here in its null-result form | all | shared-gate | — | open — norm now; pi adopts it for its own owed `cmp` list this session | pi 7 2026-09-06; rmbp 13 correction; `video/mod.rs:72`, `arroyo:5210`/`:5215` |

| SR16 | **THE ERET-SCRUB VERDICT WAS A `CPU_AUTO` KERNEL TASK, SO IT WAS STEAL-ELIGIBLE, AND IN 3/3 FAILING `kernel8-test` RUNS IT WAS STOLEN `c2->c0` AND NEVER PRINTED — the two ERET-SCRUB REQUIREs were ABSENT, not FAIL.** Measured 2026-09-16 on the Pi 4 QEMU lane at `fa4dcf0b` (loads 15–20 and 3.85) and `11ca67f1` (load 1.04): each serial carries `:: [smpbal] steal 'eret-verdict' c2->c0 (m=1) ::` after `SCHED: task 'eret-verdict' -> core 2 (policy: load-balanced, …)`, then 0 lines with `ERET-SCRUB`; gate 6 at the same `11ca67f1` under load 9.55 read 126/126. The steal is by design: `steal_ok = (requested_cpu == CPU_AUTO)` at spawn (`sched.rs` `spawn_inner`), `steal_one` honours real pins, and the `no-migrate` word on the SCHED line is printed for every task; the steal line can precede the placement line because the push precedes the print (rerun serial: steal at 472, placement at 480). Core 0 IS an online scheduling core on this lane (`run_bsp(0)` → `mark_online(0)`; `el0-eretsvc` was `CPU_AUTO`-placed on it and exited there), so the stale `m6e_verdict` comment about an unscheduled BSP was wrong. Load dependence = a race: the thief must find its own queue empty in the window between `m6e_verdict`'s push (victim floor 1, core 2 `running`) and core 2's own pop; concurrent vCPUs on a quiet host hit it, time-sliced vCPUs on a loaded host miss it. NOT established: why the stolen task produced no line on core 0 (it left core 0's queue with no `[spin6]`/`[redzone]`/`[wedge4]`/`[skill]` witness; core 0 stole again with an empty queue at serial line 730), and `u4-launch` (caller-pinned core 2, never stolen) also printed nothing in the `11ca67f1` run — carried on trunk QUEUE §5. FIX (SMPBALK8): `eret_scrub_launch` spawns the verdict on the caller's core like the M6b/M6d/M6f verdicts (`vcpu`), one existing line of `arch/aarch64/syscall.rs`, zero lines added; EL0 witnesses keep `CPU_AUTO`. Population: `awk 'index($0,"[smpbal] steal")' <serial>` = 12 lines per run (the `STEAL_LOG_MAX` cap), 1 of them `eret-verdict`. | Pi, Orin (aarch64 `syscall.rs`, shared aarch64 scheduler) | rmbp | Pi bench (`kernel8-test` is pi's leg, R39) | fixed-unflown — branch `exec-rmbp-smpbalk8` off `3e4b7aa1`; gates in the commit body | `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp-0915/smpbalk8/SMPBALK8.md`; `docs/dev/OS/02_KERNEL_CORE/scheduler.md` §SMPBALK8 |
| SR17 | **THE REPO HAD NO GATE THAT COULD SEE A REF, SO THE ONE PLACE WORK ACTUALLY WENT MISSING WAS THE ONE PLACE NOTHING WAS LOOKING.** Every gate in this tree judges FILES; an executor's work lives on a REF, which no tree contains, and the branch census (935d099a) judged 177 rows over 206 such refs to find 23 LOST, 19 of them shared code or shared docs, 14 of them from a single day when a seat hit its credit limit between the executors' reports and the fold. The registry is falsifiable in both directions: a closed line whose ref has moved REDs as stale, an IN-FLIGHT line may only move forward off its recorded cut and REDs at 14 days, a fold sha that is on no track REDs, and a LOST line whose ref later lands on a track REDs until the fold sha is written. **LOST is OWED, not RED, by default — a deliberate choice:** those 39 refs are judged, owned and printed with ref, tip and subject on every run, while the silent kind (a tip nobody has judged) is red out of the box; `UNAOS_BRANCH_STRICT=1` arms the owed ones, and arming strict by ancestry the way SR13 does is the next cut once the queues have worked the debt down. | all | rmbp (`unaos/scripts/branch-census.sh`) | — | fixed-unflown — BUILT 2026-09-22 by BRANCHGATE on `exec-rmbp-branchgate`, parent 2fc9c71c; re-derive the sha at the land. | SR18 | **EVERY PER-MOUNT `[vfs]` WITNESS ON x86 HAD BEEN DEAD SINCE X86BIND, AND THE ONLY THING CARRYING THE FACT WAS A FIXTURE.** `fs/bootdisk.rs::bind` runs PER VERB and took `announce` from a bare one-shot latch (`!MOUNTS_ANNOUNCED.swap(true, …)`). On x86 the FIRST mount table is built before any disk has enumerated — this gate's own wire shows the window: two `[vfs] root -> NONE` walks, then `[vfs] resurvey n=2 … bound_on_pass=3` — so the latch was spent on a table that printed NOTHING, and `[vfs] root mount`, `[vfs] boot mount`, `[vfs] apps mount`, `[vfs] volume mounted` and `[vfs] data mount` each read 0 lines for the whole boot on a board where four prefixes bound. Population, one command per witness: `LC_ALL=C grep -a -c -F '[vfs] root mount' target/serial.log` -> **0** on CARDROOT's two captures (2026-09-17, layout §1.6.7) and 0 again on this arc's own go-red build. What carried the fact instead was `:: volid: mount …`, a FIXTURE line — a witness certifying the wrong thing. **THE FIX IS ONE CLAUSE**: `s.root.is_some() && !MOUNTS_ANNOUNCED.swap(true, …)`, so the latch falls on the first table that BOUND A ROOT. It is deliberately NOT widened with an `s.others.is_empty()` disjunction: `survey()` caches only a walk that FOUND a root and re-walks on every fingerprint change (SO38), so a table can carry home-soil disks while the root is still unresolved, and latching there would lose the root/boot/apps/data lines one table later — the same defect in a later spelling. WHAT IT GIVES UP, said out loud rather than discovered: a board that enumerates disks and NEVER binds a root now announces no `[vfs] volume mounted` line at all; its witness is the `[vfs] root -> NONE` line the walk prints, and the mounts are still MADE either way. **THE WITNESSES ARE NOW SCORED RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT** — four REQUIREs in `scripts/specs/x86-fat.spec`, which `test-fat sf` replays as a STEP of the verb (its rc IS the verb's rc); `/volumes/data` is OPTIONAL there because the sf image carries no `DATA/` directory and a REQUIRE would pin a fact about a different image. MEASURED, `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_QUARRY=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test-fat sf 200`: rc=0, sidecar `completion=complete complete_line=2319`, `MBENCH PASS — 40/40 required witnesses, 0 forbidden hit(s), 2694 lines scanned` (the same spec asked 36 before), and the four witnesses read **1 each** — one line per bound prefix, exactly once, matching the same boot's `:: X86BIND: … mounts=4 layout=true -> PASS ::` and its four `:: volid: mount …` lines. GO-RED, behavioural on this gate: put the latch back on the first table and rebuild -> all five read 0 and `mbench.py --replay`, the single verdict authority, answers `MBENCH FAIL — 36/40 required witnesses` rc=1 at `FIRST-SHORTFALL x86-fat.spec:339`. That red run ALSO carries an unrelated compositor flake (`[dmgovlp] verdict … drag_evt=0 … adopt_stretch=1/4 -> FAIL`; the green run of the same fixture reads `drag_evt=5 … adopt_stretch=4/4 -> PASS`), and `scan_serial_faults` reports before the replay, so the spec verdict was taken by running that authority over the very capture the red run produced. Reverted. AARCH64 IS UNCHANGED AND THAT IS MEASURED, not assumed: `./arroyo test-arm` rc=0, the `[vfs]` block byte-identical before and after (2 `planwalk` fixture lines — the virt lane never reaches a filesystem verb), and the whole 658-line capture diffs to ZERO once numeric fields are normalised; the Pi and the Orin bind a root on the first table they build, where the old latch and the new one answer the same. Image cost: aarch64 `kernel.elf` 2,142,776 -> 2,142,808 B (+32), x86_64 3,027,608 -> 3,025,136 B (-2472, the early-return path re-inlining) | all (shared `fs/bootdisk.rs`) | rmbp | rMBP metal — x86 QEMU-proven here; the aarch64 boards are unchanged by construction | fixed-unflown — branch `exec-rmbp-vfswit` off `2fc9c71c`; gates in the commit body | `docs/dev/OS/09_FILESYSTEM/layout.md` §1.6.8; the VFSWIT block of `unaos/scripts/specs/x86-fat.spec` |
| SR19 | **S7'S "TWO AXES" EXPIRED WITH THE DESIGN THAT NAMED THEM: AT `cab94b9e` THE THREE RENDER MEMBERS DIFFER ON SEVEN THINGS THAT ARE NOT CONSTANTS, AND ONLY THREE STEPS OF THE WHOLE PASS ARE STILL LITERALLY IDENTICAL.** `docs/dev/evidence/orin14/S7-CONVERGENCE.md` §1 tabled the family at `6cc8de8c` and found the divergence reducible to the WAIT and INPUT OWNERSHIP plus consequences expressible as constants; §6 priced the arc against that ("~15 changed in place, ~90 added"). Re-read step by step at `cab94b9e`, sixteen days and four x86 arcs later (PTRLAG B134, PTRINSTALL2 B117, APPPIN R49, VUGPERF), the non-constant axes are SEVEN: (1) the wait; (2) input ownership; (3) **the census**, and the part a constant cannot reach is not the clock but the STORAGE CLASS — `[sched6]` reads four task-local accumulators, `[schedx86]` reads shared statics PRECISELY so a re-homed twin continues a dead instance's numbers, `[orinrender]` rate-limits on CNTPCT because a pass count is not a rate limit on a busy-poll loop; (4) **where input is ROUTED** — x86 routes at the CONSUMER (`wc_route_event` at the head of the drain, `wc_route_tail(raw)` after the arms), the Pi at the PRODUCER (`pump_usb_into_gui` consults `user_input_active()` before `gui_send`), a different pipeline stage with no Pi call to gate off; (5) **where the pointer is INSTALLED** — PTRINSTALL2 moved the x86 relative install to the producer, so the x86 `Mouse` arm is `cursor::draw_over` and must NOT call `move_rel` (a second install doubles the motion) while the Pi arm still installs, which is also why `wm::drag_route_tail` is folded onto it; (6) **the burst discipline** — x86 drains a burst per pass through an inner loop with the PTRCH motion fold and a `pending` slot that must outlive BOTH loops, and `handle_key` answering `true` stops the drain, a clause that exists only where there is a drain; (7) **the shell instance lifecycle** — the Pi's APPPIN is `shellwin_service_rearm` folded into the mint arm's own guard, x86's is a 45-line `dock::take_launch` quit/launch block with a `wm::winid_gen` generation fence and `shell_key_sink_note` (this is S7's own class note about the two S4 drains, and it is a bug fix, not tidiness). Only THREE steps are still identical across Pi and x86: the surface setup (`*WRITER.lock()` + `Screen::new`), the `console_launch_drain` call and the shell-window present. Three of the Pi body's helpers do not exist on x86 AT ALL — `click1_dispatch`, `serfocus_witness` and `shellwin_service_rearm` are each `#[cfg(all(target_arch = "aarch64", feature = "baremetal"))]`, as are `shell_inbox` and `SERIAL_WAKE_PENDING` — so a widened body needs a guard per helper, which is the cfg thicket the convergence exists to remove. **What this does NOT say:** the convergence is not wrong. RENDCONV M1 is the proof that an axis lifts cleanly when it is genuinely an axis — the wait and the census came out of the Pi body in 421 unchanged lines. It says the design's ORDER and COST are priced against a family that no longer exists, so S7 steps 2-3 are re-scoped against this table and not against §1's. | all | rmbp measured; each seat its own member | — | open — measured 2026-09-22 on `exec-rmbp-rendconv` at `cab94b9e`; M1 landed, steps 2-3 owed and re-scoped | `docs/dev/OS/08_VIDEO/engine.md` §RENDCONV; rmbp-ledger B144; `docs/dev/evidence/orin14/S7-CONVERGENCE.md` §1/§6 (the table this supersedes) |
| SR20 | **THE ON-UnaOS SMessage BUS WAS A BOARD-SPLIT OF THE PROGRAM STORY, AND THE MODULE SAID SO IN ITS OWN LAST LINE.** `crates/kernel/src/arch/aarch64/bus.rs` — 678 lines of frame layout, decode ceilings, typed verb bodies and the frozen UnaOS-NATIVE v1 goldens, naming no register, no board and no arch — closed with "no_std; aarch64-only (declared under arch/aarch64; zero x86 surface)", and `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs` dispatched neither `SYS_MSEND` nor `SYS_MRECV` (`grep` found neither number in the file). THE ABI NUMBERS WERE NEVER THE SPLIT: 19 and 20 have been in `una-abi` since ABIFREEZE and are UNCHANGED by this arc — the split was that the WIRE of ROADMAP §3b's own principle ("port the bus, not the binary convention") lived under one board's arch directory, so the rMBP desktop's three EL0 programs had no bus to speak on at all (LAWS §3: ONE OS; R16). **THE FIX FOR THE WIRE IS A `git mv` AND FOUR LINE-NEUTRAL EDITS.** The module is `crate::bus`, declared ONCE in `lib.rs` under `any(feature = "aarch64_el0", target_arch = "x86_64")` — the UNION of the two arches' EXISTING EL0 conditions, not a new knob (aarch64 keeps the gate `syscall.rs` itself is under; x86 compiles it unconditionally exactly as it compiles `SYS_OPEN`, because ring 3 is not optional there) — and `bus_codec_selftest` / `bus_codec2_selftest` now run on BOTH arches every boot instead of on the Pi alone, which is the half of "one module on both arches" that a declaration alone does not buy: MEASURED on the x86 capture, `:: BANDY-CODEC: native v1 wire frozen … PASS [w=0x3f] ::` and `:: BANDY-CODEC2: write-side wire frozen … PASS [w=0x3f] ::`, the first time either golden has been asserted off the Pi. **AND IT FALSIFIES THE OBVIOUS ASSUMPTION, WHICH IS WHY THAT IS THE NEXT THING THIS ROW SAYS: A FILE MOVE IS NOT BYTE-NEUTRAL FOR AN IMAGE THAT COMPILES THE FILE.** A module's own source path is a panic `Location` string in `.rodata`. `LC_ALL=C grep -a -o -E 'crates/kernel/src/[a-z0-9_/]*bus\.rs' target/pi_baremetal/kernel8.img` returns exactly ONE hit on each side of the move — `crates/kernel/src/arch/aarch64/bus.rs` before, `crates/kernel/src/bus.rs` after — 10 bytes shorter, so every address downstream of it shifts: `kernel8.img` `img:e675e45b…` -> `img:11a88412…`, `kernel.elf` `img:bab40d4d…` -> `img:bb819be2…`. **THE DISCIPLINE THAT MAKES THAT ONE STRING THE WHOLE DELTA**, because the checkable claim is not "identical" but "identical except for a thing that provably had to change": `arch/aarch64/mod.rs` keeps FIVE COMMENT LINES where its five-line `pub mod bus;` block stood (that file carries a ⚠ ONE LINE warning at `mod.rs:351` for exactly this); `lib.rs` takes the declaration as a SAME-LINE fold onto `pub mod sched_spread;`, its sibling lift (VUGSPREAD pulled arch-neutral POLICY out of `arch/x86_64/sched.rs` one arc earlier for the same reason); `bus.rs`'s own header edit is one line for one; and the 27 `super::bus::` -> `crate::bus::` rewrites are COLUMN-neutral because `super` and `crate` are both five characters, so not even a `Location` column moves. `git diff --numstat` is 5/5, 29/29, 1/1, 1/1 — four files, added == removed on every one. **THE KNOB-OFF MEASUREMENT SPLITS THE SAME WAY AND IS QUOTED WHOLE**, `./arroyo knoboff wc 878e1818` rc=1 `warm=yes`, control `x86 armed≠off: YES arm armed≠off: YES`: `✅ arm knob-off image BYTE-IDENTICAL to baseline — 1,601,385 B` (that leg's feature set is `[sdwrite]`, which does not carry `aarch64_el0`, so the module is not compiled there and there is no string to shift), and `❌ x86 knob-off image MOVED — 1,605,400 -> 1,619,696 B` (+14,296). The x86 move is this arc's POINT rather than its accident, and a knoboff exit 0 was never available to it: the verb scores "did the diff move the knob-off image", and for a lift whose purpose is to put code on the other arch the honest answer is yes. | all (the wire is shared code) | rmbp | the Pi battery is unchanged by construction and re-measured 126/126; the x86 half is QEMU-proven, metal owed | fixed-unflown — branch `exec-rmbp-busx86` off `878e1818`; M1 and M2 are the branch's two commits (the seat re-derives the tip at the fold); gates in the commit bodies. **M3 CLOSES THE ARC ON x86 (2026-09-22, branch `exec-rmbp-busx86m3` off `2154841b`; rmbp-ledger B171):** the ring-3 midden twin `busx86-midden` drives the DIRECT syscall and the BUS request from one private slot and compares — `:: BUSX86-EQ: … legs=9 same=9 diff=0 … -> PASS ::`, nine legs of {ls,cat,cp} × {own, another row's, absent}, errno byte-for-byte plus the cat content and the cp copy read back, write/rm/mv `-ENOSYS` distinguished from `-EINVAL` by CHANNEL, pinned in `x86-default.spec` on `diff=0` AND `-> PASS`. TWO RESIDUALS, both named and neither a bus asymmetry: (1) **x86 metal, still owed for the whole arc** — QEMU-green on both lanes, the rMBP leg rides the next attended sitting; (2) **the staging-pool ceiling M3 measured** — `NWSTAGE == 3` and `open_created_sibling` allocates its OWN slot, so verifying a bus `cp` costs three at once (source, the destination the fulfiller keeps, the read-back sibling) and one other row holding a created file open makes the read-back `-EMFILE`; the direct copy path pays the same, and the descriptor-free create that retires `busx_cp`'s honest-scope note (STOR-1's line) retires this too. **RESIDUAL (2) IS CLOSED, 2026-09-23, branch `exec-rmbp-stor1` off `f7787226`; rmbp-ledger B177.** STOR-1 M1 made a created name a TABLE ENTRY that outlives every descriptor, so M3's `busx_cp` mints its destination with `created_entry_create` and keeps NOTHING — not one of the invoker's eight handle slots, not a descriptor, and not a staging slot. `NWSTAGE` is still THREE and untouched; what changed is what it bounds, and that is the whole of the fix: it bounds CONCURRENT LIVE WRITERS again, which is what a staging pool is for, and a bus `cp` is not one. Verifying a cp cost three slots at once and costs two, the read-back of a copy nobody holds open resolving through `open_created_entry` from the entry's own twin rather than a peer's descriptor. The two-phase fixture no longer NEEDS its phases (three where it needed four) but KEEPS them, because re-ordering a `global_asm!` block mid-file is not a same-line fold (B94) — so the retirement is proved POSITIVELY by a new witness instead, `:: BUSX86-WR: … PASS [w=0x7ff/0x7ff] ::` bit10, a bus `cp` made with TWO created files held open and read back byte-exact, which is the exact shape that printed `cp-copy open_rc=-24`. **AND THE WRITE SIDE STOPPED ANSWERING -ENOSYS IN THE SAME ARC**: `busx_mv` IS `rename_created` and `busx_rm` IS `created_entry_destroy`, the same functions `SYS_RENAME` (ABI 50, matched from this bus's own `mv` body because aarch64 has no rename syscall) and the direct by-name delete call — so the write side's equivalence is BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by comparison, there being no second implementation to drift. BUSX86-EQ's own write-side pin is re-pointed in that commit from `3/3 -ENOSYS` to the exact fulfilled triple `write/rm/mv=-EBUSY/ok/-ENOENT`, because fulfilling the verbs gave that fixture's own frames an effect. **RESIDUAL (1), x86 METAL, IS UNCHANGED AND NOW OWED FOR BOTH ARCS' WORTH OF THIS WIRE** — STOR-1 is QEMU-green on both lanes (13/13 required, 0 forbidden) and knob-OFF, measured: `ops_replayed=3` is reachable only when the storage service is not up, so the on-disk half of the entry model is compiled and type-checked both ways and flown nowhere yet | ROADMAP §3b third arc; rmbp-ledger B153 and B171; `docs/dev/QUEUE.md` §4 |

| SR77 | **AETHERWSS — `wss://` was refused (SR70's hand-back): http_core `host` kept its tls_core session private, so gneiss_pal `ws::connect` answered "wss:// is owed" and no secure WebSocket (every real chat or app page) could open.** The arc: an UPGRADED-CONNECTION API in http_core `host` — `Agent::upgrade(&self, req: Request) -> Result<Upgraded, Error>` sends the request with `Connection: Upgrade` / `Upgrade: websocket`, reads the 101 head (interim 1xx skipped, any other final status an error, no redirect followed), and hands back the live stream as `Upgraded: Read + Write + Send` with every byte read past the head kept at its front; dial, proxy (CONNECT, for both schemes) and trust store as `send` uses them (the TLS setup is one `with_tls` both paths call; ALPN `http/1.1` only); gneiss_pal `ws::connect` takes `wss://` through it (`ws::connect_via` takes any scheme through a caller's Agent). No new crate. Proof: `cargo test -p http_core --features host` (`host::upgrade::tests`) and `cargo test -p gneiss_pal` (`ws_tests::client_over_agent_upgrade_keeps_early_frames`, `ws_tests::wss_is_not_refused_up_front`), `cargo test -p aether` unchanged, and a live `wss://` echo at the fold. | shared (`unaos/libs/net/http_core`, `libs/gneiss_pal`, `handlers/aether`) | rmbp | host | fixed-unflown — AETHERWSS on exec-host-aetherwss @the commit carrying this row (parent 3cf96e79): compiled (`cargo check -p http_core --features host -p gneiss_pal -p aether` rc=0), proof owed to the fold. New `host/upgrade.rs`: `Agent::upgrade` + `Upgraded` (plain: the TcpStream behind the leftover bytes; TLS: the session stays on a pump thread that drains queued writes, then peeks the socket for 5 ms and calls `Client::recv` only when a byte is readable, since tls_core fails the session on any transport timeout); `with_tls` factored out of `connection_thread`; gneiss_pal `ws::Transport for Upgraded`, the wss refusal gone; Aether's `WebSocket` unchanged. Remaining limit (tls_core, not touched): a post-handshake record arriving alone holds queued writes until the next record or the per-read timeout. | SR70 (AETHERNET), SR51 (HTTPCORE), SR58 (TLSCORE2) |

| SR88 | **AETHERCACHE — Aether's network layer spoke HTTP/1.1 only (`AgentConfig.alpn` defaulted to `http/1.1` while an `h2` path sat behind a feature, and even negotiated h2 served ONE exchange per connection at a time), had no HTTP cache at all (every subresource refetched every load, `Request.cache` ignored), and loaded subresources in fixed phases (all sheets, then images by 8, then fonts, then scripts) with no priority or dedup.** The arc: HTTP/2 by default for https (ALPN `h2` first, multiplexed streams on one connection per origin, flow control, GOAWAY/REFUSED_STREAM retried on a new connection, `Alt-Svc` ignored, coalescing of a burst onto the handshaking connection), RFC 9111 caching (freshness, age, validation, 304 merge, Cache-Control directives, Vary, partition by top-level site, unsafe-method invalidation) under the Fetch cache modes, a memory tier for the page plus a bounded LRU disk tier, and a priority loader (document > CSS > fonts > scripts > images). | shared (`unaos/libs/net/http_core`, `libs/gneiss_pal`, `handlers/aether`) | rmbp | host | fixed-unflown — AETHERCACHE on exec-host-aethercache @the commit carrying this row (parent 3cf96e79): compiled (`cargo check -p http_core --all-features -p gneiss_pal -p aether --tests` rc=0), proof owed to the fold. h2 is the default ALPN (`h2`, `http/1.1`) and multiplexes every request to an origin on one connection (TLS reads record-bounded so a socket `peek` gates the frame loop); new `http_core::cache` (no_std, RFC 9111 + Fetch cache modes, LRU memory tier, disk encoding) feeds a per-hop cache in both gneiss_pal clients (memory + disk tiers, tee-on-read, 304 freshen, stale-while-revalidate on a thread); Aether shares one cache (64 MiB memory per page, 256 MiB disk in `storage::http_cache_dir()`), passes `Request.cache` and the top-level site from JS fetch, and loads subresources through a deduplicating priority queue with RFC 9218 `Priority` fields. | SR90 | **UNAMETAL — the Una IDE has no UnaOS-native face: the Code layout (CODEX §4: Tabula centre, Midden bottom, the sidebar) exists only as the GTK vessel (vessels/una, R104 keeps it); on the glass its three cores run apart (EDITCORE SR73 has no ring-3 consumer, MIDDEN.ELF SR75 is a lone window).** The arc: `unaos/crates/user-una` → `APPS/UNA.ELF`, one 288x288 compositor window split three ways — a sidebar file tree over SYS_PATH_READ+PATH_R_LIST / SYS_STAT of the workspace (argv[1] or the session home), a centre editor over edit_core (open/save through SYS_PATH_READ/SYS_PATH_WRITE, cursor/selection/undo/search from the core, a status line with path, dirty, line:col; a `highlight` seam left for SYNTAXCORE SR89, nothing linked), a bottom console that is MIDDEN.ELF's loop copied into a module (midden_core plan, the ring-3 served set, run/bare names through SYS_SPAWN_PATH); focus by Ctrl+1/2/3 (read as the bare digit RELEASE whose press the HID decoder suppresses), Esc and click; Ctrl+S save, Ctrl+O open the selection, Ctrl+Q quit; text through font_core::ui faces off the volume, font8x8 fallback. `build_user_una_x86` in arroyo (cloned from MIDDEN's, called at its two sites, USER_CHECK_MATRIX row), the builder stages APPS/UNA.ELF; knob `UNAOS_UNA=1` → feature `una` in the five places arming `crates/kernel/src/unaapp.rs` and `tests una` (validate, spawn, Ctrl+3 + `help` into the console, Ctrl+Q, the exit status read back). | shared (unaos/crates/user-una, unaos/arroyo, unaos/builder/src/main.rs, unaos/crates/kernel/src/unaapp.rs) | rmbp | x86 QEMU, then the rmbp bench | flown — flight 31 boot 1 (ST402): `:: UNAAPP: image=/apps/UNA.ELF window=elf panes=3 lines=135 -> PASS ::`; `una` typed at the door opened the three panes on the glass, a dock tile and an app menu (FLIGHT31.md §1); UNAMETAL2 (SR98) adds save + highlight | R104, SR73 (EDITCORE), SR75 (MIDDENTWIN), SR89 (SYNTAXCORE seam) |

| SR140 | **AETHERSIDETABS — Aether had one horizontal tab strip and nothing for the many-tabs user (no groups, no reorder, no tab search), and its UnaOS-face header looked unfinished.** R118 (Peter at the flight-34 glass): "i am really liking the side tabs they recently added to chrome as i am one of those many tabs guys." He added at the glass on 2026-10-14 that the header gave "more a early x-windows vibe opposed to refined mac". The model (std-free; APPS/AETHER.ELF links it by path): `chrome/tabs.rs` adds tab groups (`TabGroup`, `GroupColor` with nine colours, `group: Option<GroupId>`). A group's tabs stay contiguous, an empty group is dropped, a collapsed group never hides the active tab, and selecting a hidden tab expands its group. It also adds `move_to` (the drag: drop between two members of a group and the tab joins it, drag clear and it leaves), `search` (case-insensitive title or URL substring, for Ctrl+Shift+A), `rows` (what the side strip lists), `top_segments` (the top strip's group bars) and `Tab::initial` (the letter tile). NEW `chrome/sidetabs.rs`: `TabLayout {Top, Side}` with the pref key, `SideStrip` (240 px wide or a 48 px rail with one toggle; `layout`, `hit`, `scroll_by`, `reveal`; 32 px rows), `TabSearch` and `is_tab_search_key`. Principia key `aether.tabs.layout` (enum top or side, default top; schema row and PREFS-SCHEMA.md row added by hand in render_markdown's shape, Rows: 56). GTK face (`libs/quartzite/src/platforms/gtk/tab_strip.rs`, R104): reads the key when the face is built and again on PrefChanged or a change to the store's folder. `bootstrap_side_tabs` puts a GtkListBox column beside the content: letter tile, ellipsised title, close button on hover, active row selected, a `+` row, vertical scroll and the rail toggle. A live switch only shows one strip and hides the other. Ctrl+Shift+A opens a tab-search window. UnaOS face (`unaos/crates/user-aether/src/chrome.rs`): the header is redrawn with every dimension named once and every colour derived once from the theme. It adds anti-aliased rounded fills and strokes in that file, stroked glyphs with hover and pressed washes, a pill-shaped omnibox with a quiet border and a focus halo, an active tab joined to the toolbar, a darker strip with hairlines, a centred letter tile and title, and a close button on hover; the header is now 56 px (was 38). Owed and not done (files outside the brief): the wire (bandy `BrowserTabs` carries no group, URL or per-tab favicon; no `BrowserTabMove` or group verbs), so the GTK face has no groups or drag yet. `mod.rs` must call `bootstrap_side_tabs(synapse, &devtools.root)` in place of `root.append(&devtools.root)`. UnaOS face (`unaos/crates/user-aether/src/{chrome.rs,main.rs}`): the page origin is `Chrome::page_x`/`page_y`, read by `logical`, `to_window`, the pointer rewrite and the blit; under `aether.tabs.layout = side` the left column is `sidetabs::SideStrip` (width capped at a third of the 288 px surface by the new `width_for`, rail, wheel scroll, reveal on every switch, group headers that collapse), re-read from Principia on every store move and every `theme::POLL_MS`; the toolbar gains reload and a menu (new tab, bookmark, bookmarks and history, downloads, find, zoom in, zoom out, actual size, close tab, settings), and a tab draws its favicon tile when the face holds one. | shared (libs/bandy, vessels/aether-shell, unaos/crates/user-aether, handlers/aether/src/chrome, libs/quartzite/src/platforms/gtk/tab_strip.rs, handlers/aether/tests/sidetabs.rs, libs/bandy/tests/smessage_kats.rs) | rmbp | host | fixed-unflown — AETHERSIDETABS2 on exec-host-aethersidetabs2 @the commit carrying this row (parent 32520f4f, the arc's first commit, parent 360dbad9): compiled (first commit: `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/root/unaos-bench/scratch/cloud/wave32/target cargo check -p aether -p aether-shell -p bandy --tests --features aether-shell/gtk` rc=0; both commits: `cd unaos/crates/user-aether && CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/root/unaos-bench/scratch/cloud/wave32/target cargo +nightly check --release -Z json-target-spec --target ../../x86_64-unknown-none.json` rc=0), proof owed to the fold. | SR147 | **TEMPLE — R128's game tier (the FULL scene as ONE picture: the zircon and its beam in the foreground over the lensed star sphere and the dark-matter flow behind, Peter's clarification "i meant the both combined") is built only if its 3D reaches a stated bar; the bar: a 960x600 still converging in 3 s or less and a 480x300 orbit at 10 fps or more, on one rMBP core.** The renderer core landed first, as the arc orders: unaos/libs/media/temple_core (no_std + alloc, CHARTER Vug, seam shared-core), a software ray tracer over a scalar trait (src/real.rs) with a Q16.16 integer backend and an f32 backend. In it: a 16-half-space zircon (the SDF max of planes, clipped in closed form with fractions compared by cross-multiplication); Schlick Fresnel; refraction in, a far face, total internal reflection, a second bounce and refraction out at three wavelengths (n 1.920 / 1.955 / 2.000); Beer absorption; the beam's single scatter as a closed-form Gaussian line integral with Lorentzian splash glows and two dispersed RGB fans; the dark-matter flow as a baked 64^3 curl field (grad a x grad b over a domain-warped noise) sampled on seven shells at their far crossings, drawn as band strokes, with its mass gradient as a multi-plane lens; three layers of cube-mapped stars looked up through the bent direction; a jittered progressive accumulator and an ACES tone table. Found, the arc's question: the x86 ring-3 target is +soft-float with SSE off (unaos/x86_64-unknown-none.json), the kernel clears CR4.OSFXSR for every UnaOS ring-3 task (crates/kernel/src/arch/x86_64/linuxabi/fpu.rs:20), and a ring-3 window is capped at 288x288 (FB_WIN_MAX_W/H); so an x86 program can run the Q16.16 backend only. Measured on the host (Xeon 2.80GHz cloud core, one thread; not the rMBP), docs/dev/evidence/temple/TEMPLE.md: fx16 still converged at 8 spp in 5594 ms against 3000, orbit 5.87 fps against 10 (the bar is NOT reached); f32 2127 ms and 14.51 fps (would reach it with an FPU context in ring 3). Lens one ray 10.4 px against a finite-difference reference at cos 0.9996; Snell to 1e-4 at all three wavelengths; fx16 tracks f32 at 0.91 LSB mean; the 64x64 fx16 checksum is 0xccb62dbc8bf87eed. Per the stop rule the vessel (crates/user-temple), the arroyo staging (which would also need the builder's fixed APPS list in unaos/builder/src/main.rs) and the design record were not built. | shared (vessels/temple, libs/) | rmbp | host, then x86 QEMU as a ring-3 program | fixed-unflown — TEMPLE on exec-host-temple @the commit carrying this row (parent 23fe38b3): measured (`cd unaos/libs/media/temple_core && TEMPLE_EVIDENCE=docs/dev/evidence/temple CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/root/unaos-bench/scratch/cloud/wave35/target cargo test -- --nocapture --test-threads=1` rc=0, 5 passed; the run before it stopped at one compile error, fixed), proof owed to the fold. The core and its measurement landed and the bar is not reached by the integer arithmetic x86 ring 3 can run (5.6 s against 3 s, 5.9 fps against 10); the game tier waits on a ruling (an FPU context for UnaOS ring-3 tasks, or more than one core). | R128, B661 (STARFIELD), B559 (PALETTECORE), R106, R107 |
| SR148 | **GEODEMETA — metadata did not survive an archive: Geode (CODEX §2, Archives) was a README with no crate, so nothing could carry a UnaFS entry's attribute set (owner, mode, every `una:*` key, small or spilled) and its three §R7 times off one volume and back onto another, and no extraction said what a FAT target loses.** `handlers/geode` (new crate, host): `src/lib.rs` is the `.geode` v1 format — header `GEODEAR1` ‖ version ‖ count, then per entry in pre-order the relative path, the §R4 kind, ctime ‖ mtime ‖ atime, the attributes as one §R1 `Map<String, AttributeValue>` written and read by `unafs::codec` (small and resolved spilled values together), and the bytes; trailer `GEODEEND` ‖ FNV-1a 64 of the body. `records`/`pack` walk a directory (ls in name order, `get_attribute` for each spilled key); `unpack` checks the whole archive (checksum, paths, parents) and every top-level name before the first write, creates, writes and sets attributes in order (the target's own `set_attribute` re-decides the small/large split identically), then restores the times last to first through the new `UnaFS::set_times` (`unaos/libs/fs/unafs/src/fs.rs`, the one write that does not stamp the clock); `unpack_plain` extracts to a host directory (a mounted FAT volume) keeping names, bytes, mtime and atime, and calls its warning exactly once before any write: `[geode] warn: <dir> keeps no UnaFS records; dropping attrs=<n> on entries=<m>/<t> keys=<k,…> and every ctime (kept: names, bytes, mtime, atime)`. Locations (inode id, parent id, extents) are not carried; the target allocates its own. Refused by name: symlinks and system objects (unafs has no public symlink create). | SR152 | **DISKRESERVE — a full UnaFS volume could refuse the delete that would empty it, and a failed write left its allocations in RAM: DISKFULL (B699) found the crate's `write_data` returned before any `txn_unwind` (only `create_files_batch`, `snapshot_create` and the commit unwound), and copy-on-write needs fresh blocks for an unlink, a truncate or a rename, with no reserve to draw them from.** M1: `unaos/libs/fs/unafs/src/fs.rs` `txn_guard` wraps every public mutation (`write_data`, `truncate_data`, `create_inode`, `mkdir`, `create_file`, `set_attribute`, `remove_attribute`, `set_times`, `unlink`, `rmdir`, `rename`; each body is now `<name>_staged`) and unwinds to the committed root when the op failed after changing a count (the new `RefMap::epoch` moved and leaves are still staged) or taking an inode id; a refusal that staged nothing unwinds nothing. M2: `unaos/libs/fs/unafs/src/refmap.rs` `reserve_for` (2 % of the volume, 64..4096 blocks, at most an eighth of a toy volume) and `allocate_data`, called only by `write_data`'s data blocks through `alloc_data_block`, refuse at the reserve while every metadata allocation spends it to zero; the refusal stays `FileSystemError::NoSpace` (every caller already maps it to ENOSPC) and `UnaFS::no_space_kind()` says `Reserve` or `Full`, carried across the unwind. M3: `UnaFS::free_blocks()` is the usable count (reserve excluded), so the kernel's `[fs] full … free_kb=` and the bar read usable space with no kernel change; the root record keeps the map's count; `tools/unafs stat` prints `volume blocks= free= reserve=`; the grow report reads the usable count. Tests (new `unaos/libs/fs/unafs/tests/reserve_logic.rs`): `failed_write_unwinds_to_committed_maps` (40-block appends until refused: kind Reserve, free count, map blocks, generation, listing, every inode's size, extents and map entry equal a mount of the committed root, both fsck clean, the next write lands) and `full_volume_still_deletes_renames_truncates` (fill to `free_blocks() = 0`, then rename, truncate, two unlinks all succeed and a 16-block write lands). Owed outside the arc's files: three existing crate tests assert the pre-reserve numbers (`tests/grow_logic.rs:115`, `tests/map_tree.rs:114` count free space across a grow without the reserve's growth; `tests/snapshot_logic.rs:565` expects a snapshot to be refused on a data-full volume, which the reserve now holds); the exact one-line changes are in the commit body. | shared (unaos/libs/fs/unafs, tools/unafs) | rmbp | host | fixed-unflown — DISKRESERVE on exec-rmbp-diskreserve @the commit carrying this row (parent b71c7fa8): compiled (`CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/root/unaos-bench/scratch/cloud/wave36/target-host cargo check -p unafs -p unafs-cli` rc=0; `cargo test -p unafs --no-fail-fast` rc=0, 243 passed, with the three owed test lines applied locally and reverted before this commit), proof owed to the fold. ## P. Process hazards (every seat) — facts, not defects; no status column, the gate ignores them

- **P1** — Agent worktrees are seeded at `main`'s tip, not the track tip. Every executor brief: `git log -1` first; `git reset --hard <tip>` on the private branch before editing *(standing)*
- **P2** — `git merge -F -` does not read stdin (write the message to a file); `unwrap80.sh` takes a FILE, not stdin *(standing)*
- **P3** — A boot's loader anchor may land in `raw.log`/`unknown.log`, not the board log; pin by anchor in whichever file carries it, then prove board purity *(standing)*
- **P4** — A peer's relay of Peter's word is a report, not the word; focus is assigned in your own session *(standing)*
- **P5** — ONE-TIME BACKLOG SWEEP, each seat: every track-named file in any plan surface (`batons/ whiteboards/ queue/ review/ wip/ active/ future/ metal/ past/`, memory, scratch — pi measured 12 surfaces, 383 files) with no closure marker and mtime > 14 days gets a ledger row or a DROPPED ruling. The ledger only accretes from the first audit forward; the rot is in what predates it *(owed (pi, rmbp) — orin swept 2026-09-05 6cc8de8c: 25 surfaces, 767 files, 355 orin-named, 193 stale, 41 unmarked → 30 already-landed, 2 dropped, 9 not-work-items, 0 open; two rows exposed on the way, C10 + S27 — `docs/dev/evidence/orin14/P5-SWEEP.md`)*
- **P6** — `affects` is NOT a judgment call: the finder states the FILE; every lane that compiles that file is affected by construction *(standing)*
- **P7** — A LINE-NEUTRAL append placed AFTER a line's trailing `//` comment is prose: it compiles nothing and the check stays green (PRTSCR-ORIN `2000608a`, and the ORINRX census at the seat's union merge — both caught by a position check at the fold). The append goes BEFORE the first `//`; prove it with `python3 -c` position check or a grep that the statement precedes `//` *(standing)*
- **P8** — `git cherry-pick --continue` commits whatever is staged, conflict markers included, if the resolver failed before `git add`; `bash -n unaos/arroyo` (and a `grep -c '^<<<<<<<'` on every unmerged file) before any continue (orin 13, a20839c6 → fixed 2e149665) *(standing)*
- **P9** — a check that runs while the tree is being edited reports the tree AT READ TIME, file by file: a knob declared in Cargo.toml and read before arroyo's leg list was resolved reds the coverage check on a state that never existed as a commit. Never edit the tree under a running check; if you did, discard the verdict and re-run on the commit *(standing)*
- **P10** — A parked artifact that does not apply is NOT evidence of loss: `git apply --check` failing says the tree differs, not in which direction. Determine direction before triage — is the change ABSENT, or already PRESENT? Check the artifact's distinctive content in the tree. Three instances in one evening (pi's S22, S23; rmbp's E2) all failed the same way: an item assumed open because nobody asked whether it had already closed. The backlog's danger is phantom obligations that cause work to be redone, not lost work (pi 6) *(standing)*
- **P11** — A backgrounded shell that `cd unaos && ./arroyo check` and then `git add unaos/scripts/…` doubles the path (`unaos/unaos/…`): the commit silently does not happen while the check reports green. Chain commits from the repo root or use `git -C <root>`; read `git status` after, the exit code does not say (rmbp 11) *(standing)*
- **P12** — `TaskStop` on a backgrounded `./arroyo check` kills the shell, not its cargo children; they keep running against the same target dir and a fresh check started beside them dies mid-run (exit 144). `pgrep -fa '[c]argo'` and kill the orphans before restarting (orin 13) *(standing)*
- **P13** — `pkill -f 'cargo'` matches the shell that issued it (the pattern is in its own command line) and kills your session's command; write the pattern as `'[c]argo'` so it cannot match itself — the same trap as the baton's `pgrep` warning about the butler (orin 13) *(standing)*


- **P14** — **A cross-ref to another seat's not-yet-folded row gates DIFFERENTLY depending on whose tree runs the check, and one of the three verdicts is silence.** With seat-prefixed ids live and GATE-LEDGER's widened resolver on only one branch, the same `→ SR1` text yields three answers: **resolves** on a tree that has both the widened resolver and the row; **REDs** on a tree with the resolver but not yet the row (any seat that adopts the gate before the fold); **silently skipped** on a tree with neither — the worst case, because the row reads as checked and is not. **Norm (pi 7, 2026-09-06, adopted): a cross-ref to a row that has not folded yet stays PROSE — "rmbp SR1 `9d92f32c`" — and becomes `→ SR1` only after the fold puts the row in the same tree.** pi 7 wrote the first one this way deliberately rather than minting a cross-ref that could not be correct in any tree. **FOURTH verdict (pi 7, found by running the widened resolver over their own file before folding the gate): a checker that scans free text cannot distinguish a MENTION of an id from a REFERENCE to one.** Every id quoted in ledger prose — go-red fixtures, examples, a commit message pasted into a cell — is live input to the resolver, so the sentence DOCUMENTING a gate's test can become a failing input to it. **AMENDED 2026-09-06 (pi 7 corrected their own account; rmbp 13 had propagated it): that was true for an ARCH ledger and INERT in `LEDGER.md` itself.** The resolver carried `and path != "docs/dev/LEDGER.md"`, exempting the over-arching ledger's own cross-refs from resolution entirely — so the `SP99` fixture that supposedly demonstrated this would have passed silently, forever. Proved by mutation on two trees: `-> S777` injected into a LEDGER.md table row passed at exit 0 while the same ref in an arch ledger red at exit 1. The exemption is now GONE, along with two siblings of it, so the claim above is true everywhere for the first time — which is the better reason to keep citing fixtures with an ASCII arrow, not a weaker one. This is S17 (prose counted as code) and P14 meeting in one line. **The escape is the arrow glyph and it is now a contract: a UNICODE arrow is a reference the gate resolves; an ASCII `->` is a mention it ignores.** No gate change was needed — the resolver already matched the Unicode arrow only, so the escape existed accidentally and is now documented at the code and in `STRUCTURAL_GATES.md` §GATE-LEDGER, whose own `-> S999` fixture citation was this tree's latent instance *(standing)* **RESOLVED IN THE GATE, not left as a norm (pi 7 found the live instance, rmbp 13 settled it, 2026-09-06).** The norm above and the id contract COLLIDE: `STRUCTURAL_GATES.md` sanctions the suffix form `A36 (-> SR2)` in the id cell, but P14 says an unfolded cross-ref stays prose, and an id-suffix cross-ref cannot be prose without breaking the id convention — so the sanctioned form lost silently. Live instance: `A36 (-> SR2)` on hw-jetson, where SR2 lives on hw-rmbp and that tree has ZERO `SR` rows in any of its three ledger files. The gate now SPLITS the verdict instead of asking seats to remember a norm: shared ids (`S<n>`, `P<n>`) live in every tree (measured: 27-31 S-rows on main, hw-jetson, hw-pi4 and hw-rmbp alike), so an unresolved one is still RED; seat-prefixed ids (`SR`/`SO`/`SP`) are branch-local by construction, so an unresolved one is **DEFERRED** — printed, counted, named in the summary, never silently skipped — and `UNAOS_LEDGER_STRICT=1` turns every deferral into a red. **The TRUNK enforces automatically** — the trigger is the branch, not an exported variable (pi 7 caught that the first cut left the backstop to a seat's memory: exactly one un-environmented call site and no landing gate command to hold the export): a track branch defers, the trunk reds, and the landing merges to trunk and runs the battery there. The sharper discriminator (defer only when the prefix has zero rows here) was rejected on purpose: it false-reds in the partial-fold window, which is the surprise this change exists to prevent. Prose cross-refs remain fine and are still the right choice in body text; the id-suffix form is now safe to use.
- **P15** — **An edit to a refusal or error message ships with the OUTPUT, not with the diff.** Formulated by pi 7 (2026-09-06) from an instance in rmbp 13, and filed here because the instance is this seat's. rmbp 13 built `./arroyo knoboff` and proved five arms — usage, undeclared feature, armed env, PASS, GO-RED — then took a request to reword a SIXTH, the control-failure refusal. pi 7's request was a question nobody had asked: **has that branch ever executed?** It had not. **Editing text on a branch is a claim that the branch produces text; a message never seen printed is unverified code, not documentation.** Making it run found TWO defects, neither visible in any diff. (1) `_knoboff_checkout` ran `git checkout --detach` BEFORE the `reset --hard` that clears the previous run's applied patch, so git aborted and the `&&` chain died — the tool worked on a session's first run and failed on every later one whose baseline differed from the throwaway tree's commit, which is all of them. (2) The new guidance was in the WRONG ARM: a `baremetal`-gated feature does not build-and-match, its armed build FAILS on Pi-only imports (`piinstall` reaches for `crate::drivers::emmc2`), so the reader landed in the other branch and was told to hunt a partner knob that does not exist. Both were found by running the branch and reading its output. **This sits one level below the round's other rule** ("a check that cannot fire must SAY so rather than return green"): that one is about a comparison with no power; this is about a branch whose EXISTENCE is untested, where the message is the only evidence it would ever emit and nobody has watched it emit. Practice: quote the printed output in the commit or the review, never the intended wording.
